# TRIPLE-BLIND VERIFICATION PASS — 2026-08-06 (operator-ordered: "triple blind findings to verify")

SCOPE ECHO-BACK (RULE TWELVE): the 31 MAJOR findings from the 1a+ adversarial review (the set behind decision
items R-1..R-11) were each re-verified by THREE independent blind verifiers. Blindness: the payload each verifier
read had the first pass's verdicts STRIPPED; verifiers could not see each other; each worked a distinct
refutation lens — (1) CODE MECHANICS (re-derive the mechanism from source: fields, units, signs, control flow),
(2) REACHABILITY (is the failure reachable under the live flag set read 2026-08-06T19:48:01Z — latent vs
live-now), (3) SEVERITY/BLAST RADIUS (assuming it fires, does the money impact follow; DOWNGRADE and UPGRADE both
permitted). Majority of 3 rules; no tie is resolved by the author. NAMED SCOPE BOUNDARY (not silently dropped):
the 61 MINOR and 55 NOTE findings were not in this pass, exactly as they were not in the first verify pass.

**RULE NINE NOTICE — NOTHING HAS BEEN DEMOTED, REORDERED, OR DROPPED.** Several findings came back DOWNGRADE on
majority vote. A new measurement that weakens an item is NOT authority to demote it: every finding keeps its
original M-number, its original position, and its place in decision items R-1..R-12. The verdict changes below
are REPORTED for the operator's ruling. Nothing has been fixed.

## 0. HEADLINE

**93 of 93 votes REFUTED nothing — the REFUTED count is 0.** Across 93 independent blind verifier votes the
tally is: CONFIRMED 70 · DOWNGRADE 23.
Per-finding majority verdicts (n=31 findings): CONFIRMED 23 · DOWNGRADE 8.
Of the 31 findings with a complete 3-vote panel, 22 were UNANIMOUS and 9 were split.

The first pass used ONE verifier per finding. This pass used three, blind. **6 findings changed verdict** (listed in §1). Not one finding was refuted by any verifier on any lens — the disagreements
are all about SEVERITY and REACHABILITY, never about whether the code does what the finding says.

## 1. DISAGREEMENTS WITH THE SINGLE-VERIFIER PASS (operator ruling input)

**M-1 SOFTENED: first pass CONFIRMED -> triple-blind DOWNGRADE** (mechanics:CONFIRMED/reachability:DOWNGRADE/severity:DOWNGRADE; severity opinions {'MINOR': 3})
  F3 ties the floor gate to _D3_FIRST_SEEN restore order: rung-0 deadlock after restart with SELECT_BUDGET=0
  kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:1320
  - [mechanics] CONFIRMED: Every link of the claimed mechanism verified line-by-line in kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py at HEAD bcca876. (1) F3 scaling: _expected_credit_usd:1321-1331 — `if D3_RAMP: ... eff_ct = float(_d3_est_ct(...)); if join_ct > 0 and eff_ct < join_ct: pc *= eff_ct / join_ct`. (2) Rung-0 on unrestored map: _d3_est_ct:625-627 — `fs = (_D3_FIRST_SEEN or {}).get(ticker); if fs is None: rung = 0`; D3_RUNGS[0]=5 (line 529-534); module-level `_D3_FIRST_SEEN = None` at :653, never restored at startup. (3) Restore path A gated by SELECT_BUDGET: _d3_first_seen_ensure (:560-578) is called at exactly one site, :5019, inside `if SELECT_BUDGET and footprint:` (:5015) + `if D3_RAMP:` (:5018); SELECT_BUDGET default 0 at :1011. Grep over kalshi_live/*.py confirms no other restore site. (4) Restore path B (belt) runs only after a non-empty q: :5322 `if q and D3_RAMP:` then :5324-5334 restores the full map from load_state(); desired_quotes for that same market has already run at :5287, and the presence/$1-floor gate inside it (:2917-2952) returns [] for a flat, non-resting market whose scaled expectation is below MIN_CREDIT_USD ($1.20 default, :1213) — so q=[] skips the belt, exactly as claimed. (5) The prune/plan writer at :6093 is guarded `if D3_RAMP and _D3_FIRST_SEEN is not None:` so it cannot restore either. The self-sustaining loop is therefore reachable: if every footprint market returns [] (rung-0-cut floor refusal), _D3_FIRST_SEEN stays None and every subsequent cycle repeats the rung-0 evaluation. The claim is actually slightly STRONGER than stated in one respect: the void/activate path applies the same floor with the same rung-0-scaled _expected_credit_usd (:3098-3110, returning [] unconditionally — no resting-order bypass there), so void books are NOT an escape hatch. The belt's own comment (:5329-5330, review #7) documents that under SELECT_BUDGET=0 the belt is the only restore path, corroborating the ordering. Escape hatches that bound the damage (mostly acknowledged by the claimant): any held inventory produces unwind quotes → belt restores the whole map from disk; any market clearing $1.20 even at 5ct (high-pool markets, or join_ct<=5 where no cut applies) produces q; and — unmentioned by the claimant — a non-void market where we still have venue-standing resting orders bypasses the floor refusal (`_resting_here`, :2933-2951) and yields q, so a restart mid-quoting with a standing book typically self-heals on the first such market. Also, for UNPROVEN series the new-series clamp (:631-635) makes the unrestored-vs-restored delta only 5ct vs 10ct (2x, not 10x); the 10x figure requires a proven (credits_n>0) rung-3 market, as the claimant's example assumes. Reachability: latent-only — live env has KALSHI_SELECT_BUDGET=1 (with D3_RAMP=1, PRESENCE_GATE=1), so :5019 restores the map before the quote loop each cycle; the defect fires only after a flip to SELECT_BUDGET=0 (its code default) plus a restart with no held inventory and no standing orders. Mechanism confirmed as claimed; severity is bounded by the latency and the multiple self-heal paths.
  - [reachability] DOWNGRADE: Mechanism fully verified in code: _expected_credit_usd scales pc by _d3_est_ct/join_ct under D3_RAMP (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:1321-1331); _d3_est_ct reads `(_D3_FIRST_SEEN or {})` so an unrestored map yields rung 0 = 5ct (:625-627, :529); _D3_FIRST_SEEN starts None (:653) and is restored only at _d3_first_seen_ensure (:5019, guarded by `if SELECT_BUDGET and footprint` :5015; SELECT_BUDGET default 0 :1011) and the quote-loop belt (:5322-5334, guarded `if q and D3_RAMP` — runs only AFTER desired_quotes returns non-empty q); the persist at :6093 skips when None. The presence/$1-floor gate (:2917-2951) refuses flat markets with _exp < MIN_CREDIT_USD, returning [] and skipping the belt. So the claimed ordering defect exists. DOWNGRADE for two reachability reasons: (1) BLOCKED LIVE — KALSHI_SELECT_BUDGET=1 in the live env, so :5015-5019 restores the map every cycle before any gate call (quote loop iterates footprint at :5209; empty footprint = no gate calls); the failure needs a flag flip, as the claimant concedes. (2) Even latent, the scenario is narrower than 'restart with no held inventory': the floor gate is entry-only — _resting_here (:2933-2938) bypasses refusal whenever we have resting orders, and a process restart does NOT clear venue-side resting orders (standing re-read from the exchange each cycle, :4180-4206, own=own_resting(standing) :4205, passed at :5288). A normal deploy-restart therefore self-heals on cycle 1: any previously-quoted market bypasses the floor, returns q, and the belt restores the global map. The true deadlock requires SELECT_BUDGET=0 AND a cold book (cancel-all/long downtime) AND no inventory AND every candidate failing the $1 floor at 5ct scaling (markets with join_ct<=5 are unscaled :1330; multi-day windows can still clear $1 :1319-1332). Damage while wedged is foregone reward only (nothing rests, no fill risk), and on-disk ramp state is preserved (:6093 no-write when None) so recovery is full-rung. Real ordering defect worth fixing (call _d3_first_seen_ensure unconditionally under D3_RAMP), but latent, compound-conditioned, self-limiting, and loss-free.
  - [severity] DOWNGRADE: Mechanism CONFIRMED in code, severity overstated. Verified chain: _expected_credit_usd scales pc by eff_ct/join_ct under D3_RAMP (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:1321-1331); eff_ct comes from _d3_est_ct which reads `(_D3_FIRST_SEEN or {}).get(ticker)` (:625) — unrestored None map => rung 0 = 5ct (D3_RUNGS :529-534); _D3_FIRST_SEEN=None at load (:653); only restores are _d3_first_seen_ensure at :5019 guarded by `if SELECT_BUDGET and footprint` (:5015; SELECT_BUDGET default 0 at :1011) and the quote-loop belt :5322-5334 guarded `if q and D3_RAMP`, which runs after desired_quotes (:5287); flat presence-floor refusal returns [] (:2938-2951, MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20 :1213). The ordering defect is real. BUT the claimed failure (restart => cycle-1 book-wide self-sustaining deadlock) overstates blast radius on four counts: (1) the floor is an ENTRY gate with a resting-orders bypass the claim omits — refusal requires `not _resting_here` (:2933-2938), and `own` is rebuilt from the venue's actual standing rows (:4205 own_resting(standing)); orders survive a normal restart venue-side (no shutdown cancel-all exists — only the deliberate STOP _flatten_all), so every previously-quoted market yields non-empty q and the belt heals the WHOLE map in cycle 1; the deadlock needs an ORDERLESS restart (post-halt flatten / long outage), a much narrower precondition than 'no held inventory'; (2) F1 makes expectation per remaining PROGRAM PERIOD (:1315-1320), so a 10x rung-0 cut still lets multi-day decent-pool programs clear $1.20 — one pass anywhere restores the full persisted map (never overwritten while None, :6093 `is not None` guard), ending the deadlock book-wide; (3) while deadlocked nothing rests => zero fill risk, pure forgone reward, and it is telemetry-visible (presence_skipped / presence_skipped_execution_only 'DEATH-SPIRAL COUNTER' :2939-2946; missing d3_ramp_tracked plan key); (4) latent-only: live KALSHI_SELECT_BUDGET=1 runs the :5019 ensure before any gate every cycle — needs a flag flip to be reachable at all. Real defect deserving a one-line fix (unconditional ensure under D3_RAMP), but latent, narrow-trigger, self-healing, opportunity-cost-only: MINOR, not MAJOR.

  Activate emitter clamps at INV_HARD_CT vs dollar-based gate — rests depth that cannot reach Target
  kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3126
  - [mechanics] CONFIRMED: Mechanism verified line-by-line. Gate limb: maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2905-2906 judges void-book bridgeability with _addable = MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL/max(best_y,best_n,0.01) (MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL=150.0 at :238) — a dollar budget that is always >= ~151ct (max(best)<=0.99) and reaches 3000ct at 5c. Emitter limb: :3115-3116 computes the full shortfall add=target-ext, :3117-3121 cost-gates the FULL shortfall against the same $150, then :3125 clamps the rested count at _acl=int(INV_HARD_CT)=80 (:1555, applied :3128/:3131 via min(int(add),_acl)). Nothing downstream restores the gap: the D3 ramp (:5322-5346, live D3_RAMP=1) only shrinks non-unwind counts further; :5988 is telemetry. So for any shortfall in (80, _addable] — a window that exists at EVERY price since _addable>=~151 — the gate passes, the emitter rests 80ct, the side stays below Target, and per the venue R3 rule every snapshot is excluded ($0 for everyone) while our 80ct carries full adverse-fill risk. The two limbs genuinely disagree on what "we can fund it" means, exactly as claimed. Reachability caveats verified: (1) the claim correctly labels this LATENT for flat entries under live PRESENCE_GATE=1 — the activate-path credit check (:3105-3110) calls _prospective_capture (:2584) -> _qualifying_score (:2536), which judges qualification on the RAW book only (:2561-2569, our prospective size never counted), so a fresh void book yields pc=0.0 (:2601-2602) -> _expA=0 < MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20 -> return [] at :3110 (EST_FEED=0 live, no floor override at :1334-1340). (2) Two claimant overstatements that do not break the finding: (a) the numeric example (shortfall 900 at 0.05 with "ext_n healthy") passes the :2906 gate only if BOTH bests are <= ~0.167, because _addable uses max of both prices — the window at healthy prices is shortfall 81-151ct, not 900; (b) the "already reachable via legacy resting activate" limb requires a pre-F1 (>80ct, pre-2026-07-29) order still resting: with own resting = exactly the 80ct clamp, raw-book qualification (ext+80>=1000 -> ext>=920) contradicts a clamp-binding shortfall (>80 -> ext<920), so only pre-clamp-era oversize orders open the window, implausible after the 08-02/08-05 halts, and the diff would self-extinguish it next cycle. Core claim (design disagreement, $0-reward 80ct fill-magnet when unmasked, currently masked by the presence-gate constant-refuse) is accurate and code-confirmed.
  - [reachability] DOWNGRADE: Mechanism CONFIRMED in code: the unqualifiable gate judges bridgeability in dollars (_addable = MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL/max(best) at maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2905-2906, $150 default :238 => e.g. 3000ct at 5c) while the activate emitter clamps per side at _acl = INV_HARD_CT = 80 (:1555, :3125-3131, F1 audit clamp comment :3122-3124). The two limbs genuinely disagree, so a passed gate can produce an under-Target rest. BUT under the LIVE flags the failure scenario is unreachable: PRESENCE_GATE=1 places the activate credit arm (:3098-3110) between gate and emitter, and _expected_credit_usd (:1292-1341) -> _prospective_capture (:2584-2620) -> _qualifying_score returns (0.0, False) whenever RAW cumulative depth < Target (:2568-2569, both sides must qualify :2601-2602). On the claimed book (ext_y=100, own=0, Target 1000) pc=0, EST_FEED=0 dark so no est floor (:1334-1340), _expA=0 < MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20 (:1213) -> gate_activate_credit return [] at :3110 — the emitter never runs, and the refusing set (raw < Target) is EXACTLY the set where the 80ct clamp would strand sub-Target depth, so the mask is complete. The claim's asserted currently-live limb ('already reachable while maintaining a legacy resting activate') is REFUTED: the activate credit arm has no _resting_here carve-out (contrast :2933-2952 on the non-void path), so an under-Target legacy rest is CANCELLED by the diff, not maintained; and when raw=ext+own >= Target the shortfall (target-ext <= own <= 50, D3 ramp rung max 50 at :529-534 applied at :5322-5346 to all non-unwind/non-macro reasons :646) never trips min(add,80). Additionally the latent magnitude is misstated: with D3_RAMP=1 live, an 'activate' quote is re-clamped at :5346 to the rung (10ct for unproven series via D3_NEWSERIES_MAX_RUNG=1 :527, max 50) — it would rest 10-50ct not 80 (worse under-bridging, smaller fill-risk dollars). Real defect class — it WAS live before the 08-02 presence-gate arming (measured 73/7080 cycles with >=1 activate, comment :3099-3104) and must be reconciled before the activate lane is revived or PRESENCE_GATE toggled off — but it is one flag/fix away, not a live loss mechanism, and the claimant's one live-reach path does not survive code tracing.
  - [severity] CONFIRMED: Mechanism verified line-by-line. Gate limb: maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2905-2906 judges bridgeability with _addable = MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL/max(best_y,best_n,0.01) — pure dollars (150.0 default at :238; live env has no override), i.e. 3000ct at $0.05. Emitter limb: :3116-3121 computes shortfall add_y/add_n and passes the cost gate whenever dollar cost <= 150 (claim's 900ct@$0.05=$45 example exact), then :3125-3131 clamps each side at _acl=int(INV_HARD_CT)=80 (:1555, no override). For any shortfall in (80, target] whose cost fits, the branch rests <=80ct that cannot reach Target; per venue R3 every snapshot stays EXCLUDED -> guaranteed $0 while the 80ct rests on a thin book. No self-correction: the unqualifiable gate re-passes identically every cycle, so the dead order is re-desired indefinitely. Masking verified: with live PRESENCE_GATE=1 the activate-credit gate (:3098-3110) constant-refuses flat void books because _prospective_capture (:2597-2602) -> _qualifying_score (:2566-2570) judges qualification on the RAW book only, returning pc=0.0 exactly -> _expA=0 < 1.20 -> []. One claim limb is overstated: 'already reachable while maintaining a legacy resting activate' is wrong — the activate-credit gate has NO _resting_here exemption (unlike the join presence gate :2936-2947), so standing activates on an unqualifiable raw book are refused and cancelled by diff_orders (:5772), not maintained; a self-qualifying >80ct legacy order would be shrunk to 80 in one cycle then cancelled, and F1-clamped code has been live since 07-29. Offsetting that, I found a piercing path the claim missed: _expected_credit_usd :1336-1342 floors expected with the venue estimate (expected = max(expected, _est)) when KALSHI_EST_FEED=1 — D-A arming is queued next, and a void-book ticker with a positive venue estimate then clears the $1.20 floor and this defect fires live with PRESENCE_GATE still ON; PRESENCE_GATE's code default is also 0 (:1211), so a bare redeploy arms it. Blast radius when armed: <=80ct/market (D3_RAMP=1 clamps lower on young tickers), notional typically <=$20/side at the low prices where the cost gate admits, realized loss capped by the $10 daily halt, activates historically ~1% of cycles (73/7080, :3100 comment) — bounded drip, but structurally guaranteed-zero-reward risk with no self-correction and a one-line coherent fix (gate should use min(_addable, INV_HARD_CT)). Latent-only under today's exact env; becomes live via the queued D-A arming or an env/default regression, which keeps MAJOR appropriate as an arm-gate item.

**M-10 SOFTENED: first pass CONFIRMED -> triple-blind DOWNGRADE** (mechanics:DOWNGRADE/reachability:DOWNGRADE/severity:DOWNGRADE; severity opinions {'MINOR': 3})
  Strand-unwind loop cancels a live resting EXIT on any transient book-read failure (no retention, unlike quote loop)
  kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5463
  - [mechanics] DOWNGRADE: The raw skeleton is real: the strand-unwind loop (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5455-5463) has `except RuntimeError: break` / `except Exception: continue` with no in-loop fetch-fail retention (unlike the quote loop's :5223-5258), and diff_orders (:3475-3492) cancels all standing orders on any ticker absent from `desired`. But the claim's neutralization analysis contains two load-bearing misreads. (1) It inverts apply_drop_grace's qualification: :3405-3407 skips a ticker only if it IS in footprint_tickers — absence from the footprint is exactly what QUALIFIES a ticker for grace (":3376-3383 'grace covers we didn't check'"). A strand ticker with standing orders, no desired entry, and not in fp_tickers is the textbook grace case, not excluded from it. (2) It asserts "DROP_GRACE not in the live flag set" — false: the ESTABLISHED live env (2026-08-06T19:48:01Z) has KALSHI_DROP_GRACE=3. So live-now: a single transient book-read failure → grace retains the standing book VERBATIM (:3385-3387, diff no-op, queue position preserved) with the reducing side re-tagged 'unwind' via held=naked_by (:3411-3418, call site :5718-5725), which cap_desired keeps unconditionally. The claimed single-timeout cancel does not occur. The flapping scenario is also wrong: grace counters reset whenever the ticker regains a desired entry (:6084-6088 — tickers absent from grace_used are not written back), so alternating fail/success cycles never exhaust grace and produce zero cancel/create churn. The only grace-exclusion route (_budget_said_no in _fp_now, :5717) cannot apply: the budget walk never refuses a held ticker (:5046-5052 sends _heldset9 straight to _keepset9), and |naked_by| >= INV_TOLERANCE implies |held_by| >= INV_TOLERANCE since naked_by=ladder_pairing(held_by) is the unmatched remainder (:4989, :7122). What survives: (a) LATENT — in a config without KALSHI_DROP_GRACE the mechanism is real as written (default 0 at :366 disables retention at :3400-3401); (b) LIVE TAIL — 4+ CONSECUTIVE strand read failures, or 4 consecutive read-budget-exhaustion breaks (the strand loop runs last in the cycle and starves first), exhaust the 3-cycle grace (:3409-3410) and the diff then cancels the live resting exit with no re-rest until a successful read; the quote-loop retention has no such cycle bound, so a persistent-failure asymmetry genuinely remains. With WS_HOT=1 + WS_BOOK_COLD=1 most books come from the WS mirror, making that sustained-failure regime narrow. The headline failure ("cancels a live resting exit on ANY transient book-read failure", flapping churn) is therefore overstated for the live deployment; the residual is a bounded-retention parity gap in the persistent-failure tail plus a latent config hazard.
  - [reachability] DOWNGRADE: Code mechanism verified as written: the strand-unwind loop (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5456-5464) skips a ticker on RuntimeError-break/Exception-continue with no in-loop retention, unlike the quote loop's explicit fetch-fail retention (:5231-5258), and diff_orders (:3475-3492) cancels everything on a ticker absent from desired. HOWEVER the claim's reachability premise — "DROP_GRACE=0, not in the live flag set" — is false: the ESTABLISHED live env sets KALSHI_DROP_GRACE=3. With that flag, apply_drop_grace (:3370-3427, called :5712-5730 before the diff) retains the strand ticker's standing book VERBATIM: it qualifies on all three conditions (has standing orders; absent from desired after the failed read; absent from _fp_now — strand tickers are by definition outside footprint per :5457, and _budget_said_no is built only from the footprint walk :5014-5087 where held tickers are unconditionally kept :5047-5052). Verbatim copies (:3411-3412) mean the diff emits neither cancel nor create — queue position PRESERVED, not destroyed. A9-F4 unwind-tagging (:3414-3421) protects the retained exit from cap_desired capital-cap drops, and the exit_only filter keeps the unwind copy. The grace counter resets on any successful cycle (:6085-6088), so the claimed flapping-feed cancel/create alternation produces zero churn. Residual live failure: only >=3 CONSECUTIVE failed reads for the same ticker (sustained per-ticker outage, or persistent strand-loop read-budget starvation via the RuntimeError break) exhausts grace and cancels a live exit on a held position — real, but far narrower than the claimed one-transient-timeout trigger. The full claimed severity is latent-only, contingent on DROP_GRACE reverting to its code default 0 (:366).
  - [severity] DOWNGRADE: The structural observation is real but the claimed live failure is neutralized by a guard the claimant missed: KALSHI_DROP_GRACE=3 IS in the established live env (claim asserts 'DROP_GRACE not in the live flag set' — false). Chain: (1) Strand loop escape paths verified as claimed — maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5458-5463, `except RuntimeError: break` / `except Exception: continue` leave `desired` without an entry, and unlike the quote loop's in-loop fetch-fail retention (:5231-5258) the strand loop has no local retention. (2) But apply_drop_grace runs AFTER the strand loop and BEFORE cap_desired/diff_orders (:5712-5725, :5745, :5772) and covers exactly this case: ticker has standing orders, absent from desired, absent from _fp_now = footprint ∪ _budget_said_no (:5717, :3376-3383, :3405-3407). A stranded held ticker is never in footprint (strand precondition :5456) and can never be in _budget_said_no because the SELECT_BUDGET walk unconditionally keeps held tickers (:5046-5052 'de-risk never gated'). (3) Retention is verbatim (:3411-3412), so diff_orders (:3479-3491) sees an exact side+price+count match — NO cancel, queue position preserved; the held/unwind tagging (:3413-3418, :5723, A9-F4 :3389-3398) keeps cap_desired from dropping it. (4) The claimed flapping scenario also fails: new_grace only carries tickers granted grace this cycle (:3399-3424), so any successful cycle resets the counter — fail-every-other-cycle never exhausts grace=3. What remains: (a) latent-only — at the code default DROP_GRACE=0 (:366, :3400-3401) the claim fires exactly as written; (b) live residual — >=4 CONSECUTIVE read failures on the same ticker exhaust grace (:3409-3410) and the exit is cancelled with no re-rest until a read succeeds; persistent budget exhaustion is itself mitigated by per-cycle budget reset (:1844,:1855-1856), PIVOT_READ_RESERVE=30 reserved for strand reads (:197,:2267-2268), and live WS book sourcing (WS_HOT=1/WS_BOOK_COLD=1); (c) a double-fault path — the grace block's own try/except (:5713-5734) skips retention if load_state() throws. Live-now money impact is bounded: one lost queue position and a short exit-less window in a multi-cycle-persistent-failure tail, backstopped by the taker cross the strand design already budgets. MAJOR is only correct for a DROP_GRACE=0 deployment; against the live flags this is MINOR.

**M-14 SOFTENED: first pass CONFIRMED -> triple-blind DOWNGRADE** (mechanics:DOWNGRADE/reachability:DOWNGRADE/severity:CONFIRMED; severity opinions {'MINOR': 2, 'MAJOR': 1})
  Venue error frame leaves mirrors CLEAN+FROZEN for the whole backoff sleep — Stage C vouches for a dead book
  kalshi_live/kalshi_ws_feed.py:271
  - [mechanics] DOWNGRADE: CORE MECHANISM CONFIRMED, MAGNITUDE REFUTED. (1) Mechanism verified line-by-line: the error-frame arm (kalshi_ws_feed.py:270-273) increments error_frames and returns False WITHOUT dirtying any mirror — unlike the seq-gap arm (:279-284, `for m in self.mirrors.values(): m.dirty = True`) and the disconnect except arm (:349-351, same dirtying). In run(), `not ok` sleeps the backoff (:334-338) BEFORE `break` (:339); the finally that clears confirmed_channels (:342-343) runs only AFTER the sleep. So for the whole backoff sleep, confirmed_channels still contains "orderbook_delta" and mirrors are clean-but-frozen (no recv during sleep). Daemon.mirror_book (maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:311-354) then passes every decline check — feed not None (:335), channel ACKed (:337), mirror not dirty (:340) — and there is a DELIBERATE no-age-bound policy (:327-331), so it vouches for the frozen book. The docstring's safety inventory at daemon :320-325 ("disconnect/error -> EVERY mirror dirty in the except arm") is factually wrong for this path: a controlled `return False` never reaches the except arm. Live-now: KALSHI_WS_BOOK_COLD=1 installs mirror_book as M.BOOK_SOURCE (daemon :369-370), heartbeat cold cycle every WS_COLD_S=60s (daemon :84, :699-708). (2) BUT the claimed "fails already elevated -> 60s backoff -> up to 60s stale" window is mechanically IMPOSSIBLE. For mirror_book to vouch, "orderbook_delta" must be in confirmed_channels, which requires the "subscribed" ack frame THIS connection (:316 clears per-connection; :265-269 adds on ack). That ack frame is itself a clean dispatch: _dispatch returns True (:269) and run() executes `self.fails = 0` (:340). Any subsequent not-ok frame breaks the inner loop immediately, so fails cannot accumulate within a connection. Therefore an error frame arriving on any vouch-capable connection always sees fails=0 -> fails=1 -> backoff = min(2.0*2^0, 60) = 2s (:334-335). Elevated fails can only coexist with EMPTY confirmed_channels (connect failures / pre-ack errors), in which case mirror_book declines at :337 — safe. The real frozen-vouched window per incident is the 2s sleep plus the reconnect-to-first-snapshot gap on the next connection (post-ack, pre-snapshot, mirror_book briefly vouches for the old frozen book again since mirrors persist on the Feed object, :209), i.e. ~2-5s total — the same order as the normal REST cold-read latency (~2-4s, daemon header :12-15) the system tolerates by design. (3) The "Stage B hot writes let through" clause is also overstated: hot_event fires only from on_book (daemon :596-608), which fires only when a frame dispatches — no frames arrive during the sleep, so no hot write can be triggered in the window. (4) Residual real defect worth fixing: the arm asymmetry and wrong docstring are genuine; a cold cycle (60s heartbeat, or a cycle_req already pending) can read ~2-5s-stale books believed fresh with ~3-8% per-incident probability, and there is an unclaimed pathological compounding case (venue repeatedly acks subscribe then errors before delivering any snapshot: mirrors never re-seed, never dirty, staleness grows across 2s-pinned cycles) — speculative venue behavior, but the code would vouch. Fix is one line (dirty mirrors, or clear confirmed_channels before the sleep, in the error arm). Severity: MINOR — real, live-now, but the reachable staleness is comparable to the REST path's own latency and the claimed 60s frozen-vouch window cannot occur.
  - [reachability] DOWNGRADE: Code observation CONFIRMED, failure scenario REFUTED as stated. Confirmed: the error-frame arm (kalshi_ws_feed.py:270-273) returns False without dirtying any mirror, unlike the seq-gap arm (:279-284, dirties all) and the exception arm (:349-351, dirties all); run() sleeps the backoff (:334-338) BEFORE break, and confirmed_channels is cleared only in the finally (:342-343) after the sleep; daemon docstring maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:322 is false for this path. BUT the claimed 60s-stale window is unreachable: (a) confirmed_channels is reset to set() immediately after connect (:316); (b) every clean dispatch — including the "subscribed" ack itself (:265-269 returns True) — resets fails to 0 (:340). Therefore fails elevated (backoff >2s) at an error frame implies ZERO clean dispatches this connection implies confirmed_channels EMPTY, and mirror_book declines at daemon :337-338 ("orderbook_delta" not in confirmed_channels -> None -> REST). Conversely, the vouching state (ack seen) implies fails was just reset, so a mid-stream error frame yields fails=1 -> backoff = min(2.0*2^0,60) = 2s exactly (:335). The claim's premise "mid-stream error frame while fails is already elevated" is structurally impossible. A seq-gap cannot substitute (dirties all mirrors :282-283; and cannot fire first-frame since _sub_seq is None'd in the finally :342, first seq seeds at :285). The Stage-B sub-claim is moot: hot_event fires only from on_book (daemon :596-608), which only runs inside _dispatch on an arriving frame (:292-298) — no frames arrive during the sleep. What remains, live-now under KALSHI_WS_BOOK_COLD=1: a few-second stale-vouch window per post-ack error frame (~2s sleep + reconnect + until fresh snapshots re-seed the carried-over mirrors after the new connection's ack), during which a timer-due cold cycle (daemon :699-708) could read books a few seconds stale — comparable to ordinary cold-cycle REST latency, not "up to 60s across ~40 books." Fix is still worth making (dirty mirrors in the error arm for consistency with its two sibling arms, and correct the daemon docstring), but the residual exposure is minor.
  - [severity] CONFIRMED: Mechanism verified line-by-line. Feed: _dispatch's error arm (kalshi_ws_feed.py:270-273) returns False WITHOUT dirtying mirrors or clearing confirmed_channels, unlike the seq-gap arm (:280-284, dirties all) and the except arm (:349-351, dirties all). run()'s not-ok path (:331-339) sleeps the escalating backoff (2..60s, :335) BEFORE break, and the finally that clears confirmed_channels/_sub_seq (:342-343) runs only AFTER the sleep — so for the whole backoff window the Feed shows the orderbook_delta ack present and every mirror clean, frozen at pre-error state. Daemon: mirror_book (maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:311-354) declines only on feed None (:335), missing ack (:337), or dirty (:340), with deliberately no age bound (:327-331) — all three pass during the sleep, so it vouches for the frozen books; its own docstring trust inventory (:322 "disconnect/error -> EVERY mirror dirty in the except arm") is false for this controlled break path. LIVE-NOW: KALSHI_WS_BOOK_COLD=1, so cold_cycle installs M.BOOK_SOURCE=mirror_book (:369-370) and run_once prices ~40 books off the frozen mirrors; with the 60s heartbeat (:84, :699-708) running independently of the sleeping feed task, a cold cycle inside a 16-60s backoff window is near-certain. Blast radius is real but bounded: (a) exposure ends at sleep end — the finally clears the ack, mirror_book declines to REST until re-ack, and the next heartbeat repositions quotes, so worst mispricing persistence ~backoff+60s; (b) 60s staleness requires fails>=6 (first error = 2s), and a pre-ack error frame is caught by the :337 gate; (c) the "Stage B hot writes let through" sub-claim is inert during the sleep — hot_event fires only from on_book (:596-608) which fires only from _dispatch, and no frames dispatch while run() sleeps (only an already-queued _exec_hot can slip through _abort_ok :510); (d) placements are post-only, so damage is adverse fills as markets trade through stale-priced resting orders plus reward loss (0.5^ticks off the moved reference), capped by per-market caps and the $10 daily loss halt. The primary claimed failure — Stage C vouching for dead books during the backoff window, live today — follows exactly as stated with the claimant's own "up to 60s" bound correct, so CONFIRMED at MAJOR (not BLOCKER: bounded window, structural self-correction, post-only, $10 halt).

**M-15 SOFTENED: first pass CONFIRMED -> triple-blind DOWNGRADE** (mechanics:CONFIRMED/reachability:DOWNGRADE/severity:DOWNGRADE; severity opinions {'MAJOR': 2, 'MINOR': 1})
  Settled-market replay-fault fallback prices all settled markets at revenue-only (cost 0), not ABSENT; fault is silent
  kalshi_live/kalshi_fill_costs.py:124
  - [mechanics] CONFIRMED: Mechanism re-derived line-by-line and holds exactly as claimed. (1) kalshi_fill_costs.py:109-123 — the `except Exception` around replay_fills(fills) increments SETTLED_REPLAY_FAILS[0] and resets cash_by/fee_by to {}, then execution FALLS THROUGH into `for s in settlements:` at :124, which is at the same indent inside the same `if replay_fills is not None:` block (:107); nothing skips it. (2) :132/:139 — realized = revenue + cash_by.get(t,0.0) = revenue (settlement payout, non-negative, cents/100 per :129), so cost_usd_day = max(0,-realized)/days = 0.0 for EVERY settled ticker, stamped settled=1 (:140), indistinguishable from a genuinely costless market; fees_usd also degrades to 0. (3) Contract violation confirmed: the counter's own comment (:31-33) documents fallback-to-ABSENT, but only the import-failure path (:104-107, replay_fills=None → settled block skipped entirely) produces ABSENT; the replay-exception path produces revenue-only rows — the claim's path distinction is exact. (4) Silence confirmed by grep: SETTLED_REPLAY_FAILS is defined (:31) and incremented (:122) and read NOWHERE else in code; main() prints only MISSING_FIELD_ROWS (:176-178) and writes only {schema, markets} (:174); the quoter's _refresh_fill_costs (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2667-2705) calls build() (:2699) and atomically replaces the file (:2700-2703) with no counter check — build() swallows the exception internally so the refresher's fail-soft except (:2704) never fires and the degraded table overwrites the good file. (5) Trigger class real: replay_fills (kalshi_attribution_ledger.py:215-234) → fill_cashflow (:229) → fill_price (:163) → fill_outcome, and fill_position_delta (:190) → fill_outcome, which deliberately RAISES ValueError on any fill lacking book_side/outcome_side (:140-152); ONE malformed fill anywhere in the tape poisons the whole replay, degrading ALL settled markets at once. (6) Consumers confirmed: kalshi_capital_rank reads cost_usd_day (:17 docstring, :37 load_fill_costs, :192 in shadow_rank) and the module/refresher comments (:156-158, quoter :2687-2688) confirm the net-EV calibration is built on this feed; the refresher runs hourly in-process (FILLCOST_REFRESH_S default 3600 at :2664, not overridden in the live env), so the code path is live today — only the trigger (venue fill-shape change) is pending. Refutation attempts failed: no outer guard sees the swallowed exception, the cash_by reset is total (no partial contamination), and no consumer or test distinguishes degraded settled=1/cost=0 rows. Severity: MAJOR — silent, contract-violating corruption of the capital-ranking and net-EV input feed, exactly the venue-rename tripwire class the module guards elsewhere (MISSING_FIELD_ROWS gets a printed WARNING at :176-178; this path gets nothing) — but it needs a trigger event before money is misallocated, so not BLOCKER.
  - [reachability] DOWNGRADE: MECHANISM CONFIRMED IN CODE, FAILURE SCENARIO OVERSTATED. (1) Fallthrough verified byte-level: kalshi_fill_costs.py:121-123 except zeroes cash_by/fee_by and execution continues into `for s in settlements:` at :124 (same 12-space indent inside `if replay_fills is not None:` :107). Every settled ticker then gets realized = revenue >= 0 (:129,:132 — settlement revenue is a payout, never negative), so cost_usd_day = 0 (:139), stamped settled=1 (:140), indistinguishable from costless. This contradicts the documented ABSENT contract (:31-33); only the import-failure path (:104-107) produces ABSENT. (2) Silence verified: SETTLED_REPLAY_FAILS exists only at :31/:122 repo-wide; main() prints only MISSING_FIELD_ROWS (:176); the quoter's _refresh_fill_costs (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2667-2705) never reads it, atomically replaces the good file (:2700-2703), and its mtime gate (:2677) guarantees the good file is overwritten within an hour of the fault — and since the bad fill stays in the historical tape, every hourly rewrite re-degrades permanently. (3) Trigger verified: fill_outcome raises on unrecognized shape (kalshi_attribution_ledger.py:140-152) and replay_fills (:215-234) has no per-fill catch, so ONE malformed fill poisons all tickers. HOWEVER, two claimed consequences fail reachability: (a) "the net-EV calibration read this feed" is FALSE in code — kalshi_netev_calibrate.py reads the transactions CSV (:18,:117,:144) and the live NETEV gate reads kalshi_netev_table.json (quoter:1366,:1382-1393,:2960); repo grep finds no netev module reading kalshi_fill_costs.json (the docstring at fill_costs.py:54 is aspirational, not implemented). (b) "the quoter keeps committing size to them" is blocked live: the feed's only in-quoter readers are _caprank_telemetry (:832-865, observation-only, receives already-final picked) and _alloc_priority (:3640-3681), which returns usd_day untouched unless KALSHI_ALLOC_KEY=1 (:3631,:3644-3645) — ALLOC_KEY is absent from the live env (default 0, BUILT NOT ENABLED per :3618-3630). What remains live-now: silent persistent corruption of the canonical feed file, the caprank shadow telemetry (the evidence base for setting CAPRANK_CALIB and arming ALLOC_KEY), and kalshi_lumps_ledger.py:28 offline analysis. The defect is real, fully silent, self-perpetuating, and becomes capital-misallocating the moment ALLOC_KEY flips (a staged roadmap enable), but today it corrupts decision evidence, not live sizing, and the net-EV linkage is factually wrong. Severity opinion MAJOR retained because silent corruption of the designated calibration/arm-gate evidence feed in a real-money system is exactly the defect-13/14 class this codebase treats as first-class harm, and it must be fixed before any ALLOC_KEY arming — but the live-now money path claimed in the scenario does not exist.
  - [severity] DOWNGRADE: MECHANISM CONFIRMED: kalshi_fill_costs.py:121-123 catches a replay_fills fault, zeroes cash_by/fee_by, and falls through into the settlements loop at :124 (same 'if replay_fills is not None:' block, :107), so every settled ticker gets realized = revenue + 0 (:132) with revenue >= 0 (settlement payout, :129) -> cost_usd_day = 0 (:139), stamped settled=1 (:140) — contradicting the documented ABSENT contract (:31-33), which only the import-failure path (:104-107) honors. Trigger verified: replay_fills (kalshi_attribution_ledger.py:215-234) has no internal try/except and calls fill_outcome (:140-152) which raises on any fill lacking book_side/outcome_side, so one malformed fill aborts the whole tape. Silence verified: SETTLED_REPLAY_FAILS written only at :122, read nowhere (grep); main() prints only MISSING_FIELD_ROWS (:176); _refresh_fill_costs (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2667-2705) overwrites the file hourly with no counter check, and re-faults every cycle (bad fill persists in the tape) — no self-correction. BUT THE CLAIMED FAILURE DOES NOT FOLLOW: (1) shadow_rank (kalshi_capital_rank.py:139, :192) looks up fill_costs only for tickers in the live candidate rows — settled tickers are never candidates, so the corrupted rows are never consulted by any ranking, and no size can be 'committed' to a settled market; (2) live markets' costs come from the positions loop (kalshi_fill_costs.py:78-96, venue realized_pnl_dollars), untouched by the replay fault, so active burners keep true costs mid-fault; (3) 'the net-EV calibration reads this feed' is false in code — kalshi_netev_calibrate.py has zero fill_costs references (reads the transactions CSV, :41/:94) and the live NETEV gate loads kalshi_netev_table.json (quoter:1365-1430); 'built on this feed' is intent commentary (:156-158), not a code path; (4) the only selection-affecting cost consumer, _alloc_priority, is gated by ALLOC_KEY default 0 (quoter:3631/:3644, not in the live flag list), and _caprank_telemetry (quoter:832-872) is structurally shadow-only. WHAT REMAINS: silent, persistent, optimistic-direction corruption of the canonical settled-market P&L record consumed today by the operator review tool kalshi_lumps_ledger.py (:28, :39-48 — settled losses vanish from family 'realized'/'worst_mkt') and intended as input to the pending netev/fill_costs calibration work, plus a doc-contract violation and a dead tripwire counter. Latent (requires a venue fill-shape change), and the same trigger crashes other tape consumers loudly (fill_outcome's crash-don't-guess contract), giving out-of-band detection. Real defect worth fixing (gate the settlements loop on a successful replay, or mark rows degraded, and surface the counter), but zero live money path consumes the wrong values under the current flags — MAJOR is overstated.

**M-20 SOFTENED: first pass CONFIRMED -> triple-blind DOWNGRADE** (mechanics:CONFIRMED/reachability:DOWNGRADE/severity:DOWNGRADE; severity opinions {'MAJOR': 1, 'MINOR': 2})
  shadow_rank commit denominator trusts cached ref with NO age cutoff — stale-extreme-ref defect lives in the alloc key
  kalshi_live/kalshi_capital_rank.py:142
  - [mechanics] CONFIRMED: CONFIRMED by independent code walk. (1) kalshi_capital_rank.py:142 reads ref from SCORES with zero age check and est_commit_usd uses it at :189 to set the cap_score denominator (:195); the 08-04 fix (:143-155) restricted prospective substitution to kind=="unknown" with ref None, so a STALE row (age>=1800s, kalshi_market_scores.py:52,229) keeps its cached ref until 7-day eviction (EVICT_AGE_S, kalshi_market_scores.py:84,101-106), and update_prospective never refreshes "ref" (only pref, :170-177). (2) Staleness is reachable: ref only updates when the market actually rests accumulating quotes (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5408-5423); an explore-probed-then-unselected market carries a days-old ref while staying in the footprint. (3) The select-budget walk was indeed fixed for exactly this class — refs age-gated at ALLOC_PCAP_MAX_AGE_S=6h before charging (quoter:5024-5033, comment verbatim as claimed) — but _alloc_priority (:3640-3678) passes raw SCORES to shadow_rank at :3667; its freshness merge (:3654-3666) only feeds the prospective dict, which stale rows never consult. (4) Magnitude verified at LIVE params (MAX_MARKET_CAPITAL=45, INV_HARD_CT=50 per KALSHI_HANDOFF_2026-08-04_EOD.md:25-26): est_commit_usd(0.50)=$45.00 vs est_commit_usd(0.95)=$21.50 — cap_score inflated 2.09x, exactly the claimed "halves est / ~2x cap_score". (Caveat: at code defaults 250/80 the est is ref-invariant; live values make it real.) (5) Downstream confirmed: alloc_prio (:4154) orders the budget walk (:5034-5036), cap_desired (:5745-5746), bound_creates (:5773), while the walk charges the age-gated est — the ordering key and charging key genuinely disagree in the same cycle. (6) Liveness: ALLOC_KEY defaults 0 (:3631) and is absent from the provided 08-06 env excerpt (known incomplete), but test_capital_rank.py:403 (committed 08-04) documents KALSHI_ALLOC_KEY=1 in the live env, and live D2=1 only has rank effect through this path (:3675-3677; the :842-857 call is telemetry-only) — so live-now with high confidence, latent-only if ALLOC_KEY was since unset. Bounding: the charge side is conservative (stale->max est) and cap_desired still backstops capital, so the damage is allocation-ordering/reward-efficiency (a stale-extreme-ref market outranks a genuinely better one), not a cap breach or direct capital loss.
  - [reachability] DOWNGRADE: MECHANISM CONFIRMED IN CODE: kalshi_capital_rank.py:142 reads `ref = (scores.get(t) or {}).get("ref")` with no timestamp check and :189 feeds it to est_commit_usd; the only age-aware branch (:156-163) applies solely to kind=="unknown" rows, so a stale row keeps its old ref. SCORES rows persist to EVICT_AGE_S=7d (kalshi_market_scores.py:84, evict :101-104), and since eviction keys on max(ts,pts,ats) (:88-98) while ref is refreshed only by update() (:143-146), a ref can outlive even 7d if sweeps stamp ats. The asymmetry vs the select-budget walk is real: the walk age-gates refs at ALLOC_PCAP_MAX_AGE_S=6h (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5024-5033, "stale -> unknown -> max est") while _alloc_priority (:3640-3678) passes raw SCORES to shadow_rank; alloc_prio consumers verified at :5034-5036 (walk order), :5745-5746 (cap_desired), :5773 (bound_creates). BUT THE FAILURE IS NOT REACHABLE LIVE, on two independent grounds. (1) ALLOC_KEY = _envi("KALSHI_ALLOC_KEY", 0) (:3631) and _alloc_priority returns usd_day verbatim when off (:3644-3645); KALSHI_ALLOC_KEY is absent from the live env list read 2026-08-06T19:48:01Z, so cap_score orders nothing live-now. The only other shadow_rank caller is _caprank_telemetry (:832-872), gated on CAPRANK_TELEMETRY (default 0, :431, also unset) and observation-only (receives final `picked`, returns nothing). The claim's own note checks out: D2 feedback acts only through this path (_load_credit_feedback gated at :484, consumed only at :842 telemetry and :3675), so live D2=1 has zero rank effect while ALLOC_KEY=0. (2) At the default sizing constants (MAX_MARKET_CAPITAL=250.0 :239, INV_HARD_CT=80.0 :1555, neither in the listed live env), est_commit_usd is REF-INSENSITIVE: per_side=125 gives int(125/p)>=126>80 for every p<=0.99, so the 80ct contract cap binds on BOTH sides and total = 80p+80(1-p) = $80.00 for any ref — the "0.95 ref halves commit, cap_score reads 2x" arithmetic only activates when the dollar cap binds on the extreme side (roughly MAX_MARKET_CAPITAL < ~$152 at ref 0.95, or INV_HARD_CT > ~132), a regime not evidenced in the given env. Verdict: real latent defect, correctly located, but the stated failure needs a flag flip (operator-gated ALLOC_KEY enable) PLUS a sizing regime where ref matters. It belongs on the ALLOC_KEY arm-gate checklist (fix the ref age gate in shadow_rank — or pass the walk's age-gated _refs9 — before enabling), not in the live-defect column. Severity opinion MINOR: latent-only, doubly conditional, but a genuine split-key inconsistency on a staged-for-enable path that should be closed before KALSHI_ALLOC_KEY=1 is named.
  - [severity] DOWNGRADE: Mechanism CONFIRMED in code: kalshi_capital_rank.py:142 reads ref from the scores row with no age cutoff and est_commit_usd(:189) divides cap_score(:195) by it, while the select-budget walk age-gates the very same refs at ALLOC_PCAP_MAX_AGE_S=6h (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5026-5033, 'stale -> unknown -> max est') and _alloc_priority age-gates only the prospective feed (:3650-3666) before passing raw SCORES to shadow_rank (:3667). Refs live to EVICT_AGE_S=7d (kalshi_market_scores.py:84,101-104). Split ordering-vs-charging key is real; consumers are the walk order (:5034), cap_desired (:5746), bound_creates (:5773). BUT the claimed MAJOR failure is overstated on every axis I could quantify: (1) impact is ordering-only — every charging path is conservative (walk charges the age-gated max est :5061, D3 ramp clamp :5067-5070 live, cap_desired/family/total caps intact), so worst case is misallocated slots = bounded reward opportunity cost, never over-commitment or realized loss; (2) it self-corrects in ~one cycle — a winner gets quoted and the fold at quoter:5420-5423 (_kms.update with y_ref) refreshes ref+ts, deflating the score next cycle; (3) magnitude measured from est_commit_usd itself: at shipped defaults (cap=250, INV_HARD_CT=80) est is ref-INDEPENDENT ($80.00 at ref 0.50/0.90/0.95, ratio 1.000 — the defect is a literal no-op); it only bites when the dollar cap binds (production-style cap=15 gives 1.71x at ref 0.95; 2.07x only at INV_HARD_CT=50), and the same row's numerator has meanwhile decayed to ~pool prior (kalshi_market_scores.py:212-215) with unknown_haircut applied (kalshi_capital_rank.py:166-167), muting how often such a row wins; (4) reachability: KALSHI_ALLOC_KEY defaults 0 (:3631) and _alloc_priority returns usd_day verbatim when off (:3644-3645); ALLOC_KEY is NOT in the established live flag list, so this is latent-or-unproven live (the D2=1 inference is plausible — D2 only matters through this path via :3675 — but is not a read). Real inconsistency worth a one-line fix (apply the same age bar inside shadow_rank), but ordering-only + conservative charging + one-cycle self-healing + config-dependent-to-zero = MINOR, not MAJOR.

### M-1 — final DOWNGRADE — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:1320
**F3 ties the floor gate to _D3_FIRST_SEEN restore order: rung-0 deadlock after restart with SELECT_BUDGET=0**

- CLAIM (unchanged from the review): _expected_credit_usd (F3) scales pc by _d3_est_ct/join_ct, and _d3_est_ct reads `(_D3_FIRST_SEEN or {})` — unknown/unrestored map = rung 0 (5ct). _D3_FIRST_SEEN is restored in only two places: _d3_first_seen_ensure at line 5019 (guarded by `if SELECT_BUDGET and footprint` — SELECT_BUDGET defaults 0 at line 1011) and the quote-loop belt at line 5324 (guarded by `if q and D3_RAMP`, which runs AFTER desired_quotes returns q for that market). The gate call inside desired_quotes therefore precedes every belt restore.

- **[mechanics] CONFIRMED** (severity opinion: MINOR)
- **[reachability] DOWNGRADE** (severity opinion: MINOR)
- **[severity] DOWNGRADE** (severity opinion: MINOR)

**Lens mechanics — CONFIRMED:** Every link of the claimed mechanism verified line-by-line in kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py at HEAD bcca876. (1) F3 scaling: _expected_credit_usd:1321-1331 — `if D3_RAMP: ... eff_ct = float(_d3_est_ct(...)); if join_ct > 0 and eff_ct < join_ct: pc *= eff_ct / join_ct`. (2) Rung-0 on unrestored map: _d3_est_ct:625-627 — `fs = (_D3_FIRST_SEEN or {}).get(ticker); if fs is None: rung = 0`; D3_RUNGS[0]=5 (line 529-534); module-level `_D3_FIRST_SEEN = None` at :653, never restored at startup. (3) Restore path A gated by SELECT_BUDGET: _d3_first_seen_ensure (:560-578) is called at exactly one site, :5019, inside `if SELECT_BUDGET and footprint:` (:5015) + `if D3_RAMP:` (:5018); SELECT_BUDGET default 0 at :1011. Grep over kalshi_live/*.py confirms no other restore site. (4) Restore path B (belt) runs only after a non-empty q: :5322 `if q and D3_RAMP:` then :5324-5334 restores the full map from load_state(); desired_quotes for that same market has already run at :5287, and the presence/$1-floor gate inside it (:2917-2952) returns [] for a flat, non-resting market whose scaled expectation is below MIN_CREDIT_USD ($1.20 default, :1213) — so q=[] skips the belt, exactly as claimed. (5) The prune/plan writer at :6093 is guarded `if D3_RAMP and _D3_FIRST_SEEN is not None:` so it cannot restore either. The self-sustaining loop is therefore reachable: if every footprint market returns [] (rung-0-cut floor refusal), _D3_FIRST_SEEN stays None and every subsequent cycle repeats the rung-0 evaluation. The claim is actually slightly STRONGER than stated in one respect: the void/activate path applies the same floor with the same rung-0-scaled _expected_credit_usd (:3098-3110, returning [] unconditionally — no resting-order bypass there), so void books are NOT an escape hatch. The belt's own comment (:5329-5330, review #7) documents that under SELECT_BUDGET=0 the belt is the only restore path, corroborating the ordering. Escape hatches that bound the damage (mostly acknowledged by the claimant): any held inventory produces unwind quotes → belt restores the whole map from disk; any market clearing $1.20 even at 5ct (high-pool markets, or join_ct<=5 where no cut applies) produces q; and — unmentioned by the claimant — a non-void market where we still have venue-standing resting orders bypasses the floor refusal (`_resting_here`, :2933-2951) and yields q, so a restart mid-quoting with a standing book typically self-heals on the first such market. Also, for UNPROVEN series the new-series clamp (:631-635) makes the unrestored-vs-restored delta only 5ct vs 10ct (2x, not 10x); the 10x figure requires a proven (credits_n>0) rung-3 market, as the claimant's example assumes. Reachability: latent-only — live env has KALSHI_SELECT_BUDGET=1 (with D3_RAMP=1, PRESENCE_GATE=1), so :5019 restores the map before the quote loop each cycle; the defect fires only after a flip to SELECT_BUDGET=0 (its code default) plus a restart with no held inventory and no standing orders. Mechanism confirmed as claimed; severity is bounded by the latency and the multiple self-heal paths.

**Lens reachability — DOWNGRADE:** Mechanism fully verified in code: _expected_credit_usd scales pc by _d3_est_ct/join_ct under D3_RAMP (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:1321-1331); _d3_est_ct reads `(_D3_FIRST_SEEN or {})` so an unrestored map yields rung 0 = 5ct (:625-627, :529); _D3_FIRST_SEEN starts None (:653) and is restored only at _d3_first_seen_ensure (:5019, guarded by `if SELECT_BUDGET and footprint` :5015; SELECT_BUDGET default 0 :1011) and the quote-loop belt (:5322-5334, guarded `if q and D3_RAMP` — runs only AFTER desired_quotes returns non-empty q); the persist at :6093 skips when None. The presence/$1-floor gate (:2917-2951) refuses flat markets with _exp < MIN_CREDIT_USD, returning [] and skipping the belt. So the claimed ordering defect exists. DOWNGRADE for two reachability reasons: (1) BLOCKED LIVE — KALSHI_SELECT_BUDGET=1 in the live env, so :5015-5019 restores the map every cycle before any gate call (quote loop iterates footprint at :5209; empty footprint = no gate calls); the failure needs a flag flip, as the claimant concedes. (2) Even latent, the scenario is narrower than 'restart with no held inventory': the floor gate is entry-only — _resting_here (:2933-2938) bypasses refusal whenever we have resting orders, and a process restart does NOT clear venue-side resting orders (standing re-read from the exchange each cycle, :4180-4206, own=own_resting(standing) :4205, passed at :5288). A normal deploy-restart therefore self-heals on cycle 1: any previously-quoted market bypasses the floor, returns q, and the belt restores the global map. The true deadlock requires SELECT_BUDGET=0 AND a cold book (cancel-all/long downtime) AND no inventory AND every candidate failing the $1 floor at 5ct scaling (markets with join_ct<=5 are unscaled :1330; multi-day windows can still clear $1 :1319-1332). Damage while wedged is foregone reward only (nothing rests, no fill risk), and on-disk ramp state is preserved (:6093 no-write when None) so recovery is full-rung. Real ordering defect worth fixing (call _d3_first_seen_ensure unconditionally under D3_RAMP), but latent, compound-conditioned, self-limiting, and loss-free.

**Lens severity — DOWNGRADE:** Mechanism CONFIRMED in code, severity overstated. Verified chain: _expected_credit_usd scales pc by eff_ct/join_ct under D3_RAMP (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:1321-1331); eff_ct comes from _d3_est_ct which reads `(_D3_FIRST_SEEN or {}).get(ticker)` (:625) — unrestored None map => rung 0 = 5ct (D3_RUNGS :529-534); _D3_FIRST_SEEN=None at load (:653); only restores are _d3_first_seen_ensure at :5019 guarded by `if SELECT_BUDGET and footprint` (:5015; SELECT_BUDGET default 0 at :1011) and the quote-loop belt :5322-5334 guarded `if q and D3_RAMP`, which runs after desired_quotes (:5287); flat presence-floor refusal returns [] (:2938-2951, MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20 :1213). The ordering defect is real. BUT the claimed failure (restart => cycle-1 book-wide self-sustaining deadlock) overstates blast radius on four counts: (1) the floor is an ENTRY gate with a resting-orders bypass the claim omits — refusal requires `not _resting_here` (:2933-2938), and `own` is rebuilt from the venue's actual standing rows (:4205 own_resting(standing)); orders survive a normal restart venue-side (no shutdown cancel-all exists — only the deliberate STOP _flatten_all), so every previously-quoted market yields non-empty q and the belt heals the WHOLE map in cycle 1; the deadlock needs an ORDERLESS restart (post-halt flatten / long outage), a much narrower precondition than 'no held inventory'; (2) F1 makes expectation per remaining PROGRAM PERIOD (:1315-1320), so a 10x rung-0 cut still lets multi-day decent-pool programs clear $1.20 — one pass anywhere restores the full persisted map (never overwritten while None, :6093 `is not None` guard), ending the deadlock book-wide; (3) while deadlocked nothing rests => zero fill risk, pure forgone reward, and it is telemetry-visible (presence_skipped / presence_skipped_execution_only 'DEATH-SPIRAL COUNTER' :2939-2946; missing d3_ramp_tracked plan key); (4) latent-only: live KALSHI_SELECT_BUDGET=1 runs the :5019 ensure before any gate every cycle — needs a flag flip to be reachable at all. Real defect deserving a one-line fix (unconditional ensure under D3_RAMP), but latent, narrow-trigger, self-healing, opportunity-cost-only: MINOR, not MAJOR.


### M-2 — final CONFIRMED — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2558
**Share model reference = best bid, not the venue's Target/5 cumulative-walk reference (LIP formula, 08-05)**

- CLAIM (unchanged from the review): _qualifying_score sets `ref = bids[0][0]` (highest bid) and applies df^ticks from THAT price; the venue-published formula defines per-side Reference Price as the first level where CUMULATIVE resting size >= Target/5 walking from best bid, with 1.0x for orders at/better than reference. When the top of book is thin (e.g. a 1-ct decoy at a high price), the venue reference sits deeper: rival orders between best bid and the true reference earn 1.0x from the venue but get df^n<1 from the model — the rival denominator is systematically understated. Also the model total stops accumulating at the Target cut, while the venue scores all resting orders on a qualifying side.

- **[mechanics] CONFIRMED** (severity opinion: MAJOR)
- **[reachability] CONFIRMED** (severity opinion: MAJOR)
- **[severity] CONFIRMED** (severity opinion: MAJOR)

**Lens mechanics — CONFIRMED:** Mechanism verified line-by-line in the worktree (HEAD bcca876). (1) Reference anchor: _qualifying_score sets `ref = bids[0][0]` unconditionally after a descending sort (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2555-2558) and scores every level `df**round((ref-price)/TICK)` from THAT price (:2561-2563, TICK=0.01 at :1565); _qualifying_breakdown does the identical walk (:2731-2736); the pinned scorecard replica's own docstring says "reference = highest bid" (kalshi_market_scorecard.py:27-28, ref at :32). The venue LIP formula given as ESTABLISHED in this task defines the per-side Reference Price as the first level where CUMULATIVE resting size >= Target/5 walking from best bid, 1.0x at/better. Grep for any Target/5 (or 0.2*target) walk across kalshi_live returns nothing — no module implements the venue reference. The two definitions coincide only when the top level alone holds >= Target/5; on thin-top books the venue ref sits deeper and every rival between best bid and the true ref is venue-scored 1.0x but model-scored 0.5^n, understating the denominator and overstating our share — anti-conservative, exactly as claimed. (2) Truncation sub-claim: both walks break at cum >= target (:2566-2567, :2739-2740) and an order below lowest_q scores 0 (:2579); the ESTABLISHED formula qualifies orders at SIDE level ("orders qualify ONLY if their side reaches Target Size") with no per-order cumulative cutoff, so excluding deep rivals is a second same-direction (share-inflating) divergence. This half rests on the briefing's rendition of the venue text rather than code, but the code behavior itself is verified fact. (3) Failure-scenario arithmetic re-derived: rival 1ct@0.45 over 1000ct@0.40, Target 1000 → venue ref=0.40 (cum 1001>=200 at level 2), rival total 1001 at 1.0x; model ref=0.45, 1000ct scores 0.5^5*1000=31.25, total 32.25. A ~50ct join at 0.45 (consistent with _capped_join, :2327-2350) models raw 50/32.25→share raw/(1+raw)≈0.61 vs venue 50/1051≈4.8% — the claimed "~60% vs ~5%" is exact. Minor overstatements that do not change the verdict: the capture gate consumer is latent (CAPTURE_GATE default 0 at :284, not in the live flag set), and the aggregate NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT=3.0 (:1363) plus the model's documented "RELATIVE signal only" M7 caveat (:2591) absorb some aggregate scale error — but a scalar haircut cannot correct a book-shape-dependent bias, which corrupts exactly the cross-sectional ranking the model is trusted for, and the presence floor uses the un-haircut expectation. ### M-3 — final CONFIRMED — kalshi_live/w0b_payout_timing.py:92
**0b tags BEFORE_CLOSE vs the event's LATEST close — multi-market events can mint a false timing canon**

- CLAIM (unchanged from the review): Credits are per EVENT (reason string carries only the event ticker), but delta is computed as credit_ts − max(close_time over ALL the event's markets). A credit that is in fact close+1 for an individual market that closed earlier than the event's last market lands at delta<0 and is printed as 'BEFORE_CLOSE ... direct evidence payment is NOT gated on close'. The (mkts N) count is printed but the summary tallies and the explicit BEFORE_CLOSE discriminator section do not distinguish single- from multi-market events.

- **[mechanics] CONFIRMED** (severity opinion: MAJOR)
- **[reachability] CONFIRMED** (severity opinion: MAJOR)
- **[severity] CONFIRMED** (severity opinion: MAJOR)

**Lens mechanics — CONFIRMED:** Mechanism verified line-by-line in kalshi_live/w0b_payout_timing.py. (1) Credits are event-keyed only: line 40 CREDIT_PREFIX="Liquidity Incentive for event ", lines 76-78 extract the event ticker; no market-level attribution exists in the script. (2) The comparison close is the event's LATEST market close: line 84 sorts all close_times from /markets?event_ticker=EV, line 85 stores closes[-1] as the second tuple element, line 99 binds it as close_max, line 100 computes d_h = credit_ts - close_max. (3) Programs are market-keyed (line 141 reads p["market_ticker"] from /incentive_programs), so a close+1 payment for an early-closing sibling market in a multi-market event yields d_h < 0 and is tagged BEFORE_CLOSE (lines 101-102) — indistinguishable from genuine pre-close payment. (4) The mitigating (mkts N) count is printed per row (line 105) but is dropped exactly where the conclusion is asserted: the summary tallies (lines 109-112) and the explicit BEFORE_CLOSE discriminator section (lines 113-116, banner "payment not gated on close — the 0b discriminator") make no single- vs multi-market distinction, matching the claim precisely. (5) Blast radius as stated: the script writes no state, but its printed BEFORE_CLOSE verdict is the 0b timing canon input, and KALSHI_FARCLOSE_PAYING_EXCEPTION=1 is live NOW — it admits markets closing weeks past program end on exactly this payout-timing assumption, so a spurious BEFORE_CLOSE ratification would understate cash-lock on live admitted markets. Kalshi events with staggered per-market closes are a real shape, so the trigger is not hypothetical. Nothing in the file or its consumers neutralizes the mechanism; the claim neither overstates (it correctly frames this as evidence-layer corruption, not direct P&L loss) nor understates the failure.

**Lens reachability — CONFIRMED:** Mechanism verified line-by-line in kalshi_live/w0b_payout_timing.py. (1) Credit attribution is event-only: line 40 CREDIT_PREFIX and lines 76-78 parse only an event ticker from the reason string — per-market attribution is impossible from this feed. (2) Lines 83-85 fetch ALL markets of the event and store (closes[0], closes[-1], len(mkts)); line 99 unpacks only close_max — the min close closes[0] is captured and then never used anywhere in the file (grep-verified), so line 100's delta is credit_ts − MAX(close_time). (3) Lines 101-103 tag delta<0 as BEFORE_CLOSE, and lines 18-19 (docstring) + line 114 hard-code the interpretation 'direct evidence payment is NOT gated on close'. For a multi-market event with staggered closes, a genuine close+1 payout for an early-closing market lands before close_max and is falsely printed as the discriminator — exactly as claimed. A sound discriminator (ts < closes[0]) was one variable away and unused. (4) The masking detail also checks out: per-row line 105 prints (mkts N), but the summary (lines 110-112) and the BEFORE_CLOSE discriminator section (lines 113-116) omit both the market count and the close spread, so the quotable evidence block carries no single-vs-multi distinction. Reachability: this is an offline operator-ordered evidence tool, ungated by env flags, and it fires on any credit for a multi-market event with heterogeneous closes — no guard exists to block the false tag. The downstream consumer is live policy: KALSHI_FARCLOSE_PAYING_EXCEPTION=1 is on in live.env and the tool's stated purpose (docstring lines 4-9) is to settle exactly the cash-lock/timing question that exception rests on, so a mis-minted canon feeds a live admission path and the D-A checkpoint interpretation. Severity bounded below BLOCKER because the script cannot trade or lose money directly — the harm is corruption of a decision-critical evidence input — hence MAJOR.

**Lens severity — CONFIRMED:** Mechanism verified line-by-line in w0b_payout_timing.py: credits are extracted per EVENT ticker (lines 76-78; corroborated by maker_kalshi_client.py:424-425 — reason is "per EVENT, never per strike"), the event's market closes are sorted and stored as (min, max, count) at lines 84-85, and line 99 unpacks `_, close_max, nmk` — discarding the earliest close — so delta at line 100 is credit_ts minus the LATEST close across all the event's markets. Lines 101-103 tag delta<0 as BEFORE_CLOSE, and the tool's own output (docstring lines 18-19, printed line 114) asserts BEFORE_CLOSE = "direct evidence payment is NOT gated on close — the 0b discriminator". For any multi-market event with staggered closes, a credit that is actually close+1 (or program-end) for an early strike lands at delta<0 vs the later strike's close and is falsely asserted as the discriminator; the summary tallies (110-112) and the BEFORE_CLOSE cases section (115-116) drop the (mkts N) count, which appears only in the per-row listing (line 105). Blast radius: no automated consumer — main() writes no state — so the failure is human canon-ratification; but that path is live-relevant because the tool exists expressly to settle the FIX-H cash-lock question (docstring lines 4-9) and KALSHI_FARCLOSE_PAYING_EXCEPTION=1 is ON in the live env (quoter :3229 flag, :3232-3244 _farclose_paying_keep, consulted at :2172 and :4144), admitting markets that close weeks past program end; line 162 even prints per-market "cash-lock if close+1: Nd", making this output the direct decision input. Severity MAJOR stands: canonical decision-tool epistemic defect feeding a live admission policy, but human-mediated with no direct automated money path (not BLOCKER) and its confounded tag is the tool's single stated discriminator (not MINOR). Valid discriminators are indeed only single-market events or per-market credit attribution, as the claimant states.

### M-5 — final CONFIRMED — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2559
**Reward model anchors the Reference Price at the best bid, not the venue's Target/5 cumulative-depth level**

- CLAIM (unchanged from the review): The venue-published LIP formula (08-05, ESTABLISHED canon in this task) defines the per-side Reference Price as the first level where CUMULATIVE resting size >= Target/5, with 1.0x distance-mult for anything at/better than that level. `_qualifying_score` (:2555-2581) and `_qualifying_breakdown` (:2718-2741) instead set `ref = bids[0][0]` (best bid) and penalize every deeper level by DF^ticks-from-best-bid. Grep confirms NO module in kalshi_live implements a Target/5 walk; kalshi_market_scorecard's own docstring says 'reference = highest bid'. Rivals resting between the true reference and the best bid are venue-scored 1.0x but model-scored 0.5^n, so book_total is understated and our share overstated. This basis feeds the LIVE $1.20 floor gate (PRESENCE_GATE=1 via _expected_credit_usd), the SCORE_RANK cache, the D2 feedback basis, the net-EV model fallback, the sweeper, and every capture_usd_day telemetry row. It also inverts the throttle-step economics: a quote 1 tick behind a thin touch can still be at/better than the true reference (full 1.0x venue credit) while the model believes it halved.

### M-7 — final CONFIRMED — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2558

The venue formula (ESTABLISHED 08-05) sets the per-side Reference Price at the first level where CUMULATIVE resting size >= Target/5, and pays 1.0x to everything at-or-better than THAT. On any book whose top level holds < Target/5, rival size (and our own resting orders' rest_df subtraction, :2575, :2772) sitting between best and the true reference is under-counted by the model but fully scored by the venue — denominator too small, our share and capture_usd_day too big.

**Lens mechanics — CONFIRMED:** Mechanism re-derived line-by-line and it holds. (1) `_cancel_each` (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:6414-6427) cancels inside try/except-pass and returns only a success COUNT — the identity of a failed cancel is discarded. (2) `_flatten_all` pass 1 calls `n += _cancel_each(client, cancels)` at :6572 and then the creates loop (:6577-6586) places every diff-emitted offset unconditionally via `create_quote(..., post_only=True)`; there is no `cancelled_ok` set, no failed-cancel-ticker/side deferral, and no re-read between cancel and create. post_only cannot block the stack because the stale twin is SAME-side (both reducing-side bids), and the per-run nonce (:6506) guarantees the venue's client_order_id dedup never rejects the fresh create. (3) The stacking scenario is concrete: stale NO 20@0.55 in `_std`, desired NO 20@0.60 → diff_orders keys on (side,price,count) so it emits cancel(stale)+create(new); the dedup loop :6556-6571 finds no keeper (stale ≠ desired key) and adds nothing; the cancel 429s silently → 40ct reducing rests against a 20ct position. (4) The through-flat consequence is the codebase's OWN stated hazard: `_unwind_size`'s contract (:2353-2358) — "resting > |inv| would, on a full fill, cross THROUGH flat and open the OPPOSITE position" — and the main-cycle C7 guard (:5797-5843) exists precisely to prevent this identical failed-cancel/same-side-unwind stack ("a second unwind stacks to ~2x|inv| and a full fill crosses THROUGH flat", :5800-5804). That guard is main-cycle-only; under STOP the run returns before the normal quoting path (:3961-3974), so it never applies. (5) The pass-1/pass-2 asymmetry is exactly as claimed: pass 2 goes through `_taker_cross_capped` → `_cancel_ticker_resting_confirmed` (:6668-6670, :6197-6233), which re-reads the venue book and REFUSES to cross unconfirmed — pass 1 has no analogue. (6) Corrective-window check: if both stacked orders fill during pass 2's ≤STOP_ESCALATE_S (90s default, :1684) wait, the residual poll (:6601-6613, :6621-6622) sees the flipped naked position ≥STOP_TAKER_MIN_CT and taker-crosses it back — bounded ~90s exposure + double spread. But in the branch where ONE of the two fills first (position flat), pass 2 exits early and returns "done" (:6610-6613, :6626-6628) WITHOUT cancelling the surviving twin; that orphan rests on a flat book and a later fill opens a naked opposite position with no correction until the next flatten, paced at STOPFLAT_REPEAT_S=1800s (:1199, :3969-3973) — matching the claimed failure window. No neutralizing guard found anywhere on the path. Severity: MAJOR, not BLOCKER — the flip is bounded at |pos| per ticker per stale order, pass 2 self-corrects the double-fill-during-wait branch within ~90s, and the next flatten's pass 1 rests a reducing offset for the flipped position at ≤1800s; it requires a conjunction of cancel failure + adverse sweep. (1) Mechanism verified line-by-line: the error-frame arm (kalshi_ws_feed.py:270-273) increments error_frames and returns False WITHOUT dirtying any mirror — unlike the seq-gap arm (:279-284, `for m in self.mirrors.values(): m.dirty = True`) and the disconnect except arm (:349-351, same dirtying). In run(), `not ok` sleeps the backoff (:334-338) BEFORE `break` (:339); the finally that clears confirmed_channels (:342-343) runs only AFTER the sleep. So for the whole backoff sleep, confirmed_channels still contains "orderbook_delta" and mirrors are clean-but-frozen (no recv during sleep). Daemon.mirror_book (maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:311-354) then passes every decline check — feed not None (:335), channel ACKed (:337), mirror not dirty (:340) — and there is a DELIBERATE no-age-bound policy (:327-331), so it vouches for the frozen book. The docstring's safety inventory at daemon :320-325 ("disconnect/error -> EVERY mirror dirty in the except arm") is factually wrong for this path: a controlled `return False` never reaches the except arm. Live-now: KALSHI_WS_BOOK_COLD=1 installs mirror_book as M.BOOK_SOURCE (daemon :369-370), heartbeat cold cycle every WS_COLD_S=60s (daemon :84, :699-708). (2) BUT the claimed "fails already elevated -> 60s backoff -> up to 60s stale" window is mechanically IMPOSSIBLE. For mirror_book to vouch, "orderbook_delta" must be in confirmed_channels, which requires the "subscribed" ack frame THIS connection (:316 clears per-connection; :265-269 adds on ack). That ack frame is itself a clean dispatch: _dispatch returns True (:269) and run() executes `self.fails = 0` (:340). Any subsequent not-ok frame breaks the inner loop immediately, so fails cannot accumulate within a connection. Therefore an error frame arriving on any vouch-capable connection always sees fails=0 -> fails=1 -> backoff = min(2.0*2^0, 60) = 2s (:334-335). Elevated fails can only coexist with EMPTY confirmed_channels (connect failures / pre-ack errors), in which case mirror_book declines at :337 — safe. The real frozen-vouched window per incident is the 2s sleep plus the reconnect-to-first-snapshot gap on the next connection (post-ack, pre-snapshot, mirror_book briefly vouches for the old frozen book again since mirrors persist on the Feed object, :209), i.e. ~2-5s total — the same order as the normal REST cold-read latency (~2-4s, daemon header :12-15) the system tolerates by design. (3) The "Stage B hot writes let through" clause is also overstated: hot_event fires only from on_book (daemon :596-608), which fires only when a frame dispatches — no frames arrive during the sleep, so no hot write can be triggered in the window. (4) Residual real defect worth fixing: the arm asymmetry and wrong docstring are genuine; a cold cycle (60s heartbeat, or a cycle_req already pending) can read ~2-5s-stale books believed fresh with ~3-8% per-incident probability, and there is an unclaimed pathological compounding case (venue repeatedly acks subscribe then errors before delivering any snapshot: mirrors never re-seed, never dirty, staleness grows across 2s-pinned cycles) — speculative venue behavior, but the code would vouch. Fix is one line (dirty mirrors, or clear confirmed_channels before the sleep, in the error arm). Severity: MINOR — real, live-now, but the reachable staleness is comparable to the REST path's own latency and the claimed 60s frozen-vouch window cannot occur.
