### M-17 — final CONFIRMED — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2558
**Model reference price = best bid; venue LIP reference = Target/5 cumulative walk — capture/share computed vs wrong ref**

- CLAIM (unchanged from the review): The venue-published LIP formula (08-05, ESTABLISHED per task context) defines the per-side Reference Price as the FIRST LEVEL where CUMULATIVE resting size >= Target/5, walking down from best bid. `_qualifying_score` (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2558 `ref = bids[0][0]`), `_qualifying_breakdown` (:2731), and kalshi_market_scorecard.qualifying_share (:32) all set ref = highest bid unconditionally. No Target/5 walk exists anywhere in kalshi_live (grepped). When top-of-book cumulative depth < Target/5 (200ct on a 1000ct-target program — common in exactly the thin books the thesis targets), the venue reference sits DEEPER than best bid, so rival orders between best bid and the true reference earn 1.0x from the venue but are discounted df^ticks by the model. The rival denominator is under-counted, our share over-estimated — anti-conservative.

- **[mechanics] CONFIRMED** (severity opinion: MAJOR)
- **[reachability] CONFIRMED** (severity opinion: MAJOR)
- **[severity] CONFIRMED** (severity opinion: MAJOR)

**Lens mechanics — CONFIRMED:** CONFIRMED by direct code re-derivation. (1) The cited code does exactly what the claim says: maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2555-2558 (`_qualifying_score`) sets `ref = bids[0][0]` — the highest bid — unconditionally, then scores every level `(df**n)*size` with n = ticks below that best bid (:2561-2563). The observation twin `_qualifying_breakdown` does the identical walk (:2728-2736), and kalshi_market_scorecard.qualifying_share (scorecard file, `ref = bids[0][0]` in the qualifying_share body, ~line 33) is the pinned byte-equivalent original. I grepped all of kalshi_live for any Target/5 cumulative-walk reference computation (target/5, target*0.2, etc.) — none exists in non-test code. The venue-published LIP formula (08-05, ESTABLISHED per briefing) defines the per-side reference as the first level where cumulative resting size >= Target/5 walking from best bid, with 1.0x at/better than reference. (2) Direction is strictly anti-conservative, not merely noisy: whenever depth at best < Target/5 the venue reference sits deeper than best bid; every rival level then has model weight = venue weight x df^(gap) (levels between best and true ref get df^n<1 in the model vs 1.0x from the venue; deeper levels get an extra df^gap), while our own join-at-best order gets 1.0x in both regimes. So the model denominator is strictly smaller and our modeled share strictly larger. I re-ran the claim's scenario numerically (0.30x50 / 0.29x300 / 0.28x700, T=1000, df=0.5, us 100ct at best): model rival total 375 vs venue 700; model share 0.211 vs venue share 0.125 — a 1.68x over-estimate (claim said ~2x; same order, slightly overstated arithmetic, immaterial). (3) Live reachability confirmed against the stated env: PRESENCE_GATE=1 consumes it at :2917-2918 via `_expected_credit_usd` (:1292), which calls `_prospective_capture` (:1316) -> `_qualifying_score` (:2597-2599) with NO model haircut (expected = ideal x presence_factor, :1332-1333; presence_factor only shrinks for execution, not model bias), and gates flat entries against MIN_CREDIT_USD=$1.20 (:1213, :2938-2951). An inflated share therefore admits thin-top-book markets whose venue-true period credit is sub-$1 (pays $0 under the program floor) while resting real size — exactly the W10 drive-by class the claim names. NETEV_GATE=1's unproven-family fallback also consumes it (:3021-3024, signal = pc/3.0 - $5.0, open iff > 0), so inflated pc can flip an unproven family from skip to open. The score cache/SCORE_RANK consumer (:2191, :415) and sweeper pcap (:2645-2661) also carry the same biased number, though SCORE_RANK/SWEEP flags were not in the stated live list (latent there). (4) Partial mitigants keep this from BLOCKER, not from CONFIRMED: the divergence vanishes when the best-bid level alone carries >= Target/5 (thick-top books match the venue exactly, ref==best), the NETEV path divides by the M7-fitted 3.0 haircut whose empirical 2-6x over-prediction already absorbs some of this bias in aggregate, D3_RAMP=1 clamps admitted size to 5-10ct initially, and the $10 daily loss halt bounds the fill-risk downside. But the error is book-shape-dependent, so no scalar haircut restores correct relative ordering, and the presence floor — the one absolute-dollar use of the model — has no haircut at all. Severity: MAJOR — live-now anti-conservative admission/ranking bias on exactly the thin books the strategy targets, bounded in dollar damage by ramp sizing and the daily halt.

**Lens reachability — CONFIRMED:** Mechanism verified in code. (1) Ref = best bid unconditionally: maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2555-2558 (_qualifying_score sorts bids desc, `ref = bids[0][0]`), :2728-2731 (_qualifying_breakdown), kalshi_market_scorecard.py:26-36 (qualifying_share, "reference = highest bid"). Grep of all kalshi_live/*.py finds no Target/5 cumulative walk anywhere — the venue's ESTABLISHED LIP reference (first level with cumulative size >= Target/5) is not implemented. (2) Direction verified one-way anti-conservative: our join is scored at best bid with DF^0=1 (:2589) and the venue also gives 1.0x at/better-than-reference, so our numerator is equal under both; every rival level's model weight df^(ticks from best bid) <= venue weight df^(max(0, ticks from deeper Target/5 ref)), so the rival denominator is under-counted and modeled share >= venue share, strictly greater whenever top-of-book depth < Target/5 (the claimant's ~2x worked example checks out). Bias is nil only when the top level alone holds >= Target/5 — but the quoter's own comment (:2900-2901, "5 of 8 of our own allowlisted programs had one side at ZERO depth against target=1000") shows thin-top books are the norm in the target universe. (3) REACHABILITY: live-now on two confirmed paths. PRESENCE_GATE=1 (live env) -> :2917-2919 _expected_credit_usd -> :1316 _prospective_capture -> :2597-2600 _qualifying_score; the $1-floor compare at :2938 uses MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20 (:1213) with NO model haircut on this path — _presence_factor (:1267-1277) is measured uptime, not a bias correction, and the venue-estimate floor EST_FEED (:1334-1340) is dark (KALSHI_EST_FEED=0). A 20% floor margin cannot cover a ~2x share inflation, so sub-$1 true-credit markets (W10 drive-by class, program pays $0) can pass exactly as claimed. The SCORE_RANK score-cache leg of the claim is conditional — KALSHI_SCORE_RANK defaults 0 (:388) and is absent from the authoritative live flag list (a :1010 comment suggests live=1; unpinned) — but the finding stands on the two confirmed-live gates. (4) No neutralizing guard: the bias loosens gates; the capture gate (:3045) consumes the same inflated pc, the selection gate (:3061-3067) tests spread/symmetry only, and the smart-throttle (:2510-2514) embeds the same ref=best assumption. Severity MAJOR, not BLOCKER: exposure bounded by the $10 daily halt and D3 ramp 5-10ct clamp (:1308-1311), NETEV path carries a 3x haircut, and the bias vanishes on deep-topped books — it admits marginal markets and distorts ranking rather than corrupting execution or accounting.

**Lens severity — CONFIRMED:** CONFIRMED. ### M-18 — final CONFIRMED — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2566
**Scored set truncated at cumulative Target — rivals below the walk cutoff contribute zero to the share denominator**

- CLAIM (unchanged from the review): _qualifying_score and _qualifying_breakdown stop accumulating `total` when cum >= target (:2566-2567 break, :2739-2740) and score our order 0 if below lowest_q (:2579). The LIP spec as given qualifies orders at SIDE level (side reaches Target => orders on that side qualify; short side => none) and normalizes score = size x distance-mult per side — it contains no per-order cutoff at the cumulative-Target boundary. Deep rivals that the venue counts at 0.5^ticks are excluded entirely from the model denominator, so our modeled share exceeds the venue share whenever meaningful size rests just past the Target boundary near the reference.

- **[reachability] CONFIRMED** (severity opinion: MAJOR)
- **[severity] CONFIRMED** (severity opinion: MAJOR)

The claim's own qualifiers are accurate: aggregate bias is absorbed into the fitted HAIRCUT=3.0 (:1363, fitted on the unshaped model per :2616-2618) but the distortion is book-shape-dependent (only ~1-3 ticks of behind-boundary depth matter given 0.5^ticks decay), so cross-sectional ranking between thick- and thin-boundary books is systematically wrong in a way no scalar corrects. Severity MAJOR not BLOCKER: harm is over-admission and mis-ranking in live money-affecting gates (real fill risk taken for reward that will not materialize as modeled), but bounded by the distance decay, partially haircut-absorbed in aggregate, and involves no crash or direct guaranteed loss.

**Lens severity — CONFIRMED:** Mechanism verified in code: _qualifying_score walks bids and breaks the instant cum >= target (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2561-2567), so all rival size past the boundary level is excluded from the share denominator; the twin walk _qualifying_breakdown has the identical break (:2739-2740) and is pinned to never drift (:2726-2727), and our own order below lowest_q scores 0 (:2579-2580). The venue-published LIP formula (ESTABLISHED) qualifies at SIDE level with 0.5^ticks distance penalty and per-side normalization — no per-order cumulative-Target cutoff — so deep-but-near rivals the venue scores at 0.5^ticks are absent from the model denominator. The failure arithmetic checks: 1000ct at ref + 5000ct one tick behind → model composed share 50/1050=4.76% (raw/(1+raw) at :2603) vs venue ~50/3550=1.41%, a ~3.4x over-estimate; the bias is one-directional (denominator only shrinks) and grows with boundary-adjacent size. Blast radius under the LIVE env: (1) PRESENCE_GATE=1 — _expected_credit_usd (:1316-1317) feeds the $1.20 floor gate on both join (:2917-2952) and activate (:3105-3110) paths with NO haircut, so the over-estimate is purely permissive and admits exactly the W10 sub-$1 drive-by class the code itself names (:2613); (2) NETEV_GATE=1 — every unproven family hits the model fallback pc/3.0−5.0 live each cycle (:3021-3024). No self-correction: the haircut is a scalar fitted on aggregate over-prediction (:1363, :2616-2618) with no shape term, so thick-boundary books are over-admitted while thin-boundary books are pc/3 under-estimated (false skips) — cross-sectional errors in both directions, defeating the model's documented 'RELATIVE signal only' role (:2591); the EST_FEED floor can only raise expectations (max at :1339-1340) and is dark. One precision note: the continuous-ranking channel the claim mentions is latent-only (sweeper KALSHI_SWEEP_ENABLED default 0 :2626-2628; ALLOC_KEY off :3644), so live the cross-sectional harm flows through binary gate admits/skips rather than a rank — but two live-money gates consuming a structurally shape-biased input with no corrective term sustains the finding as claimed. Not BLOCKER: downside bounded by the $10 daily halt, per-market caps, and the model already being treated as untrusted; dominant cost is misallocated presence and $0-credit fill risk at dollars/day scale. Caveat consistent with the INFERRED label: correctness of the claim rests on the ESTABLISHED spec being complete (no unstated per-order depth cutoff); judged against that canon the divergence is real and largest exactly at the near-boundary levels in dispute.

### M-28 — final CONFIRMED — kalshi_live/kalshi_drift_detect.py:150
**Windowed detectors treat candle index as minutes; 15.3% of window steps are gaps so W-minute windows span hours**

The timeline drops candles lacking both book and price (detect.py:85-87), leaving holes. **Lens mechanics — CONFIRMED:** Mechanism verified line-by-line in kalshi_drift_detect.py and kalshi_drift_eval.py, and the gap statistics independently re-measured on the frozen data. (1) Index-as-minutes: `_val_at` (detect.py:150-152) computes `j = i - back_min` — a pure list-index offset. D2 `det_adverse_exitable_drift` uses `j = i - W` directly (detect.py:166-169); D6 `det_run_persistence` iterates `range(i-m+1, i+1)` over the last m LIST ENTRIES (detect.py:232-234); D8 `det_spread_widen` medians `range(i-W, i)` (detect.py:249-251); D3/D4 sum tape over `range(max(0,i-W+1), i+1)` timeline indices (detect.py:176-180, :191-194). All treat one timeline entry as one minute. Notably D5/D5c are the exception — they window by wall time `t0 = t1 - W*60` (detect.py:207-210, :217-219) — and the claim correctly omits them. The eval.py score analogues repeat the index pattern: s_mid_drift (eval.py:56-58 via `D._val_at`), s_exit_drift `j = i - W` (eval.py:61-63), s_tape_share/net (eval.py:66-82), s_spread `range(i-W, i)` (eval.py:99-103). (2) Holes are real: build_timeline drops a candle only when it has neither book close nor price close (`continue` at detect.py:86-87), and the frozen cache has such holes. I re-ran the harness's own make_state over kalshi_drift_cache.json + load_positions (67/67 states) and measured consecutive in-window timeline steps: 741/4827 = 15.4% non-60s, max gap 3540s (59 min), 36/67 positions affected — matching the claim's 750/4893 = 15.3% / 3540s / 36-67 to within window-boundary counting convention. So a W=6 'minutes' look-back genuinely spans up to ~1 hour of wall time at gap points. (3) Group-differential bias direction verified: gap-step rate is 8.7% for positives vs 2.1% for temp_control (and 17.7-20% for the neg groups) — window inflation does differ by group as claimed, flattering detector TPR on positives relative to the within-TEMP control. (5) decision_points (detect.py:331-343) IS ts-aware (CYCLE_S gating on ts), so only the detectors' trailing windows are affected — exactly the scope the claim states. ### M-30 — final DOWNGRADE — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5229
**Read-budget exhaustion mid quote loop cancels the unread tail's standing book, including held-ticker exits**

- CLAIM (unchanged from the review): public_get raises RuntimeError at READ_BUDGET (200). The quote loop's `except RuntimeError: break` (:5229) bypasses the fetch-fail retention (:5232-5258) that protects standing orders on transient errors, and the strand-unwind loop breaks on the same RuntimeError (:5461). Every footprint market not reached before exhaustion is then absent from `desired`, and diff_orders(:3475) cancels ALL its standing orders - including reason-tagged exits on held tickers (which are in fp_tickers, so the strand loop skips them even when it does run). Next cycle rebuilds at the back of the queue.

- **[mechanics] DOWNGRADE** (severity opinion: MINOR)
- **[reachability] DOWNGRADE** (severity opinion: MINOR)
- **[severity] DOWNGRADE** (severity opinion: MINOR)

**Lens mechanics — DOWNGRADE:** The plumbing is real but the load-bearing consequence ("diff_orders cancels ALL standing orders of every unreached market") is neutralized under the live flag set by a guard the claim never mentions — DROP GRACE. Scope collapses from "the tail of the resting book" to one ticker per exhausting cycle (plus sustained-exhaustion teardown after 3 cycles), and it is latent-only today.

WHAT I CONFIRMED (verbatim from source)
1. `public_get` refuses at the ceiling: `if _reads[0] >= READ_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE: raise RuntimeError("read budget exhausted")` — maker_kalshi_quoter.py:1855-1856; `READ_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE = _envi("KALSHI_READ_BUDGET", 200)` at :1844; counter reset once per cycle at :3999.
2. `_get_book` propagates it unchanged (:1887-1909, docstring :1890-1891 "Raises exactly what public_get raises"). The WS mirror (BOOK_SOURCE, live KALSHI_WS_HOT=1/WS_BOOK_COLD=1) can answer without spending a read (:1895-1907), so only mirror-declined tickers can trip it.
3. The quote loop's break exists exactly as cited: `except RuntimeError: break  # budget exhausted — stop fetching` at :5229-5230, and it does bypass the fetch-fail retention block at :5231-5271 (which is the code that copies `standing[t]` into `desired[t]` and unwind-tags the reducing side, :5243-5249).
4. The strand-unwind loop breaks on the same error: :5458-5461.
5. `diff_orders` does cancel everything for a ticker present in `standing` and absent from `desired` (:3481-3488 — `want` is empty, so every `have` key is appended to `cancels`).

WHAT REFUTES THE STATED FAILURE
6. DROP HYSTERESIS runs BEFORE the diff: `desired, grace_used = apply_drop_grace(standing, desired, _fp_now, ...)` at :5712-5725, live `KALSHI_DROP_GRACE=3` (declared :366). `apply_drop_grace` retains a ticker's book VERBATIM when it has standing orders, is absent from `desired`, and is absent from the footprint set (:3405-3424); the copies carry the same side/price/count (:3411-3412), so `diff_orders` emits neither cancel nor create — pinned by test_drop_grace.py:34-41 ("retained verbatim -> no churn at all").
7. The unreached tail IS absent from that footprint set under the live PIVOT_SELECT=1: `footprint = consumed` at :5437-5438, and `consumed` only receives markets actually tried (:5218-5219). `_fp_now = {m["ticker"] for m in footprint} | _budget_said_no` (:5717), and `_budget_said_no` is the SELECT-BUDGET *capital* walk's refusal set (:5014 declaration, populated :5073/:5078, assigned :5087) — it has nothing to do with the read budget. So read-unreached tickers are grace-eligible, and their books survive the diff untouched for up to 3 consecutive cycles.
8. The claim's parenthetical "including reason-tagged exits on held tickers (which are in fp_tickers...)" is a misread under PIVOT_SELECT=1: `fp_tickers = {m["ticker"] for m in footprint}` is built from the COLLAPSED footprint (:5443), and the comment at :5434-5436 says so explicitly ("A held ticker still sitting in the UN-consumed pool tail is picked up by strand-unwind ... it is absent from fp_tickers"). Held tail tickers therefore keep their exits two ways: grace re-tags the reducing side `reason="unwind"` (:3413-3418, called with `held=naked_by` at :5723) and `cap_desired` keeps any unwind-bearing ticker unconditionally (:3731-3736), while the breaker filter also preserves reducing quotes (:5586-5597).

WHAT SURVIVES (the real, smaller defect)
9. Exactly ONE ticker is genuinely exposed: `consumed.append(m)` happens at :5219 BEFORE the fetch at :5224, so the market that trips the RuntimeError is inside `_fp_now` and grace denies it as "checked-and-rejected" (:3406, safety comment :3380-3383). With no `desired` entry (the break skipped :5231-5271), diff_orders cancels its whole book; and if it is held, the strand loop skips it precisely because it IS in fp_tickers (:5456) — so a held boundary ticker can spend a cycle with no resting exit. One ticker per exhausting cycle, not the tail.
10. Grace is bounded: `if used >= grace_cycles: continue` (:3409-3410) with the counter persisted at :6087-6088. Under exhaustion sustained across 4+ cycles the same tail ticker is released to the diff and torn down — the claim's outcome, delayed three cycles.
11. The strand-loop break (:5461) delays NEW exits on unfootprinted held tickers while the budget is spent; existing exits are preserved by grace. Delay, not teardown.
12. Config-latent worse case: with KALSHI_DROP_GRACE=0 (the module default, :366) or PIVOT_SELECT=0 (footprint never collapses, so the tail sits in fp_tickers), the claim holds in full. Not the live configuration.

REACHABILITY: latent, not live-now. Read pressure is real — the clock prefilter alone may spend `min(FOOTPRINT_TOP*4, max(40, READ_BUDGET//2))` = 100 reads (:2095) and the pivot pool is capped at `READ_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE - PIVOT_READ_RESERVE` = 170 (:2267-2268, reserve 30 at :197), plus per-naked-ticker settle reads (:5135) — so 200 is crossable once the universe widens. The claimant concedes it is not binding on the current pilot, and I could not verify the "close_unchecked_tail nonzero on 08-06 warmup" measurement from code (it is the :2108 counter, which only fires when rows exceed the 100-read prefilter budget).

**Lens reachability — DOWNGRADE:** All paths below are in C:/Users/samwa/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--lockes-picks-polymarket-ai-v2/5dfe0ebf-2821-475d-946c-72012db34c3b/scratchpad/kalshi-wt/kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py unless noted.

WHAT IS TRUE (verified):
1. public_get raises RuntimeError once _reads[0] >= READ_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE (:1854-1856), budget = 200 default (:1844, KALSHI_READ_BUDGET not in the live env list), reset per cycle at :3999. _get_book re-raises it verbatim (:1887-1909).
2. The quote loop's `except RuntimeError: break` (:5229-5230) sits ABOVE the fetch-fail retention block (:5231-5271), so the retention/unwind-tagging at :5234-5258 is indeed bypassed. CONFIRMED.
3. The strand-unwind loop breaks on the same RuntimeError (:5458-5461), not `continue`. CONFIRMED. Worse than stated in one respect: it is a `break`, and _get_book answers from the WS mirror for FREE when it vouches (:1895-1907; daemon installs Daemon.mirror_book as BOOK_SOURCE under WS_BOOK_COLD=1, maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:368-370), so both loops abandon mirror-servable tickers that would have cost zero budget.
4. diff_orders cancels every standing order on a ticker absent from `desired` (:3481-3488). CONFIRMED in isolation.

WHY THE STATED FAILURE IS OVERSTATED — two live guards the claim does not account for:

A. The claim asserts the unread tail "are in fp_tickers, so the strand loop skips them". That is FALSE under the live PIVOT_SELECT=1. `consumed.append(m)` happens per iteration (:5218-5219) and `footprint = consumed` collapses the footprint to markets actually TRIED (:5437-5438), so fp_tickers (:5443) contains only the reached prefix. The unread tail is NOT in fp_tickers.

B. Because the tail is absent from fp_tickers, DROP HYSTERESIS catches it. KALSHI_DROP_GRACE=3 live; DROP_GRACE = _envi("KALSHI_DROP_GRACE", 0) at :366; apply_drop_grace runs BEFORE the diff at :5711-5725 and retains the tail's standing orders VERBATIM (:3405-3424), which is diff-neutral — no cancel, no create, queue position and time-on-book preserved (docstring :3385-3387). For a held ticker it re-tags the reducing side reason='unwind' (:3413-3418), and cap_desired keeps any market carrying an unwind quote UNCONDITIONALLY (:3709-3714), so the tight-capital drop path is closed too. Critically, the exclusion set `_fp_now = {...} | _budget_said_no` (:5717) does NOT cover read-budget refusals: `_budget_said_no` is populated only by the SELECT_BUDGET capital walk (:5014, :5040, :5073, :5078, :5087), and those tickers are filtered out of `footprint` entirely at :5086, so they are a disjoint set from the read-budget tail.

WHAT SURVIVES (the real, reduced defect):
- Exactly ONE ticker per exhaustion event is torn down immediately: the boundary market, which was appended to `consumed` at :5219 BEFORE its _get_book raised. It is therefore in fp_tickers → grace refuses it (:3406 `t in footprint_tickers`) → the strand loop skips it (:5456) → diff cancels its whole standing book, including a reason-tagged exit if it is held. That is the claimed failure, at 1 ticker/cycle, not "the tail".
- The tail teardown does happen, but on the 4th consecutive unreached cycle when grace expires (:3408-3410, counter persisted at :6087-6089), not "the first post-exhaustion cycle".

REACHABILITY UNDER THE LIVE FLAGS — not reachable today:
Per-cycle read ceiling = programs pages ≤5 (:4065-4071, usually 1) + market-clock prefilter ≤ min(FOOTPRINT_TOP*4, max(40, 200//2)) = 100 (:2095, :2107-2110) + pool books ≤ pool_cap = min(PIVOT_POOL_MULT*FOOTPRINT_TOP, len(rows), READ_BUDGET-PIVOT_READ_RESERVE) = min(120, len(rows), 170) (:195, :197, :2267-2268) + a handful of L3 close reads (:3340) + settle checks (:5135, TAKER_FLATTEN not in the live env). The claim's "held-ticker marks" is wrong as a read cost — _held_cost (:7023) goes through the authed client, not public_get, so it spends no budget. At the current pilot universe the post-prefilter pool is ~allowlist rows + PROBE_MAX_SLOTS=5 (:519-520), i.e. ~38 book reads, total ≈140 < 200. Every book the WS mirror answers is free (:1895-1907; up to 80 subscribed tickers, daemon :225, :692, :717), pushing it further down. Exhaustion needs the widened ~3,300-row universe the claimant himself names as a precondition — a config change, i.e. LATENT, not live-now. (close_unchecked_tail being nonzero only proves the prefilter hit its own 100-read sub-budget at :2107; it does not imply the 200-read cycle budget was exhausted.)

NET: mechanism real in code, but the stated blast radius (whole tail torn down on the first post-exhaustion cycle, held exits lost) is blocked live by PIVOT_SELECT's footprint collapse plus DROP_GRACE=3; what remains is one boundary ticker per exhaustion event immediately and the tail after 3 grace cycles — and exhaustion itself is unreachable at the current universe size. Latent + reduced ⇒ DOWNGRADE, MINOR. Caveat I could not verify from code: KALSHI_FOOTPRINT_TOP, KALSHI_READ_BUDGET, KALSHI_PIVOT_POOL_MULT and KALSHI_SERIES_ALLOW are absent from the supplied live env list; I assumed the file defaults (60 / 200 / 2) and the ~33-row pilot allowlist. If FOOTPRINT_TOP or the allowlist is materially larger in the real live.env, reachability moves toward live-now and the boundary-ticker teardown (still 1/cycle) becomes a real recurring cost.

**Lens severity — DOWNGRADE:** MECHANISM CONFIRMED, STATED FAILURE OVERSTATED BY ~THE WHOLE DROP_GRACE LAYER.

WHAT I VERIFIED AS TRUE:
1. public_get raises RuntimeError("read budget exhausted") at maker_kalshi_quoter.py:1855-1856 once _reads[0] >= READ_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE (:1844, default 200). _get_book (:1886-1908) propagates it verbatim, and RuntimeError there is budget-ONLY: urllib faults are URLError/HTTPError, JSON faults ValueError, and a BOOK_SOURCE fault is caught and downgraded to "declined" at :1897-1900. So the branch separation is sound.
2. The quote loop's `except RuntimeError: break` (:5229-5230) does precede the `except Exception` fetch-fail retention (:5231-5258), so the retention that protects standing on transient errors genuinely does NOT run on exhaustion. Confirmed.
3. The strand-unwind loop breaks on the same error at :5460-5461, so no reducing quote is rested for any held ticker at or after the break point. Confirmed. (It is even a slight over-break: _get_book would still have served WS-mirror-vouched books for free at :1893-1902, but `break` forfeits those too.)
4. diff_orders (:3475-3491) iterates `set(standing) | set(desired)` and cancels every standing order whose (side,price,count) key is absent from desired. It is polarity-BLIND — a resting reason='unwind' exit is cancelled exactly like an accumulating quote. `standing` is the FULL venue resting book (_live_standing :7281-7287 -> client.get_orders("resting")), so nothing scopes the cancel to the footprint. Confirmed.
5. The read-budget accounting gap the claim implies is real: pool_cap subtracts PIVOT_READ_RESERVE=30 from the FULL 200 at :2266-2268 (min(2*FOOTPRINT_TOP=120, len(rows), 170)), while the market-clock prefilter at :2095 may ALREADY have spent min(FOOTPRINT_TOP*4, max(40, READ_BUDGET//2)) = up to 100 reads. 100 + 120 = 220 > 200. The reserve is computed against a budget that is not there.

WHAT REFUTES THE STATED FAILURE ("the first post-exhaustion cycle tears down the tail of the resting book"):
6. KALSHI_DROP_GRACE=3 is LIVE. apply_drop_grace (:3370-3424, called at :5712-5725) retains VERBATIM every standing ticker that is (a) absent from `desired` and (b) absent from this cycle's footprint_tickers (:3405-3407). The unread tail is precisely case (b): with PIVOT_SELECT=1 (live) `footprint = consumed` at :5438, and a market is appended to `consumed` only when the loop actually reaches it (:5218-5219) — so every market past the break point is absent from _fp_now (:5717) and IS grace-eligible. Retained copies are identical dicts, so diff_orders sees an exact key match and emits neither cancel nor create: the tail's book stays, queue position and all. The claim's headline event does not happen on cycle 1, 2, or 3.
7. The held-exit sub-mechanism is additionally protected: apply_drop_grace re-tags the reducing side 'unwind' for held tickers (:3413-3418, `held=naked_by` at :5723), and cap_desired keeps any market containing an 'unwind' quote UNCONDITIONALLY ahead of the capital walk (:3731-3736). bound_creates only bounds creates, never cancels (:3762-3784). So a retained tail exit cannot be dropped by the capital cap either.
8. The claim's own sub-premise is inverted: it says tail held tickers "are in fp_tickers, so the strand loop skips them even when it does run". Under live PIVOT_SELECT=1 they are NOT in fp_tickers (:5438, :5443) — the strand loop at :5455-5456 explicitly targets exactly those tickers. The stated reason the exits are unprotected is wrong; they are unprotected only by the budget break itself, and grace covers them anyway.
9. "every torn ticker loses queue position (the thing LIP pays for)" is factually wrong against the venue-published formula: score = size x 0.5^ticks distance multiplier, normalized per side, sampled at 1s snapshots. Queue position within a level is not a term in LIP. The real reward cost is absence of resting SIZE during the gap — which grace prevents for the tail.

WHAT ACTUALLY SURVIVES:
10. Exactly ONE ticker per exhausted cycle is genuinely torn down: the wall-hitter. It is appended to `consumed` at :5219 BEFORE the try/raise, so it lands in fp_tickers and _fp_now, making it grace-INELIGIBLE by design (:3406-3407, "grace covers 'we didn't check', never 'we said no'"). If that ticker is held, its resting exit is cancelled by diff_orders and the strand loop skips it (`t in fp_tickers`, :5456), so it spends the cycle with no resting exit and no replacement. That is the claimed failure, scoped to 1 market, not "the tail".
11. SUSTAINED exhaustion (>3 consecutive cycles with the same tail unread) DOES produce the claimed teardown: `used >= grace_cycles` at :3409-3410 releases the ticker to the diff, and the strand loop still cannot re-price it. This is the real residual and the more serious shape — but it needs 4+ consecutive exhausted cycles on a stable pool ordering.
12. Compounding on the wall-hitter: the settle-check taker backstop reads public_get(/markets/{t}) at :5133 and, on an exhausted budget, RuntimeError is swallowed by its bare `except Exception` into plan["settle_check_failed"] (:5136-5140) with near_settle=False — so the settlement backstop is also blind in the same cycle. Bounded by _preclose_naked_flatten (:6745+) and DAILY_LOSS_HALT_USD.

REACHABILITY UNDER THE LIVE FLAGS: latent-only, not live-now, and the claimant concedes it ("Not binding on today's 33-row pilot"). SERIES_ALLOW filters BEFORE `rows` is built (:2010-2019) and KALSHI_ALLOW_PROBE_EXCEPTION defaults to 0 (:519 "0 = allowlist absolute"), so a pilot universe of ~33 rows caps prefilter reads at ~33 and pool_cap at 33 — total well under 200. Firing requires all of: (a) a wide universe (allowlist widening or ALLOW_PROBE_EXCEPTION=1 admitting probe rows), (b) a COLD close cache so the prefilter actually spends its 100 (steady state is ~zero: positives persist across restarts via st["close_cache"] :6091 / _close_cache_restore :1714-1727, 6h TTL with 3h jitter, so ~3,300 rows amortize to single-digit re-reads per cycle), and (c) fewer than FOOTPRINT_TOP=60 markets qualifying inside the first ~100 book reads, since :5216-5217 breaks the loop cleanly once 60 are quoted. The claimant's close_unchecked_tail>0 evidence is consistent with (a)+(b) (that key is only set when _checked >= 100 at :2106-2109), but it is prefilter-budget evidence, not evidence the quote loop ever hit 200.

SEVERITY: MINOR, not MAJOR. Real code defect (the reserve arithmetic at :2266-2268 ignores the :2095 prefilter spend, and the :5229 break is the only fetch-failure path with no retention), worth fixing before the allowlist widens. But under live flags it cannot fire; if it fired, blast radius on cycle 1 is one ticker's book (possibly one held exit for one cycle) rather than the tail; the tail is fully covered for 3 cycles by a mechanism that already exists and is armed; and the money framing (queue position as the LIP payout term) is incorrect. Quantified ceilings from code: MAX_MARKET_CAPITAL $250 (:239), INV_HARD_CT 80 ct (:1555), DAILY_LOSS_HALT_USD $20 default / $10 live. Not a BLOCKER; not an ARM GATE. I would keep it as a pre-widening fix item (make the RuntimeError branch fall through to the :5232-5258 retention instead of `break`, and net the prefilter spend out of PIVOT_READ_RESERVE).


### M-31 — final CONFIRMED — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5070
**D-C (dark): budget walk charges macro ticker ~$5 while its ladder commits ~$30/side; partial ladder can rest**

- CLAIM (unchanged from the review): The walk's est for a macro ticker takes `min(est_commit, _d3_est_ct)` (:5067-5070) - macro tickers are exempt from the ramp at SIZING (_d3_apply_ramp :646) but NOT at the walk's charge, so a designated market is admitted against ~$5 of budget while _macro_probe_quotes (:681-724) intends up to MACRO_PROBE_USD/2 per side. A partial ladder does not meet Target - the side never qualifies, so the probe pays nobody while carrying fill risk: exactly the state _macro_probe_quotes refuses to create atomically (its unaffordable arm rests NOTHING). **Lens mechanics — CONFIRMED:** I re-derived every step from source in C:/Users/samwa/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--lockes-picks-polymarket-ai-v2/5dfe0ebf-2821-475d-946c-72012db34c3b/scratchpad/kalshi-wt/kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py (HEAD bcca876). The claim reads the code correctly at every cited line.

STEP 1 — the walk charges a macro ticker the RAMP number, and no macro exemption exists there. maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5061-5082 is the SELECT_BUDGET walk body: `_est9 = _kcr.est_commit_usd(ref, MAX_MARKET_CAPITAL, INV_HARD_CT)` (:5061), then the explore clamp (:5063-5066), then `if D3_RAMP: _est9 = min(_est9, float(_d3_est_ct(_t9, now.timestamp())))` (:5067-5070). There is no `MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS` branch anywhere in the walk (:5015-5101). I grepped every occurrence of "macro" in the 7,317-line file: :646, :675-724, :2004-2009, :2041-2050, :2146, :2863-2870, :3881-3889, :5361 — none is inside the walk, cap_desired, bound_creates, or the order-apply block. D3_RAMP=1 live, so the min fires. `_d3_est_ct` (:617-636) returns D3_RUNGS[rung] with defaults [5,10,25,50] (:529-534); a freshly designated macro ticker is absent from `_D3_FIRST_SEEN` -> rung 0 -> 5, and even after 10 min (D3_RUNG_S=600) the unproven-series clamp D3_NEWSERIES_MAX_RUNG=1 (:527, :631-635) holds it at 10. Charged as dollars at the $1/ct convention (:5069 comment). So $5 (claim's "~$5") to $10.

STEP 2 — the asymmetry is real and in the harmful direction. `_d3_apply_ramp` (:639-650) skips clamping when `o.get("reason") not in ("unwind", "macro_probe")` (:646) — macro_probe quotes are explicitly NOT ramp-limited, per the comment at :641-644. So the walk charges a rung the sizer will never apply to this market's quotes. Meanwhile `_macro_probe_quotes` (:681-724) sizes to `need = target - ext_rivals` (:700-702) with a 3-level ladder and per-side cap `side_cap = min(MACRO_PROBE_USD/2.0, 0.9*HELD_MAX_USD)` (:697). The claim's "~$30/side" is not a misread of the default: the D-C test fixture itself raises HELD_MAX_USD to 40.0 precisely so the $60 default is reachable ("arm both knobs coherently", test_dc_macro_probe.py:35-37), giving side_cap=$30. Arithmetic at that armed config (target 1000, 10ct external): counts=[397,298,298] via :711-713, cost=0.03*397+0.02*298+0.01*298=$20.85/side, ~$41.70 for the market — vs $5-10 charged in the walk. 4-8x under-read. (At literal defaults HELD_MAX_USD=20 -> side_cap=$18 -> a target-1000 ladder returns [] as unaffordable, so the mechanism needs the coherent arming the operator must do anyway; at target=300, KXTRUMPENDORSEMENTS, cost is ~$6.36/side and it fires even at defaults.)

STEP 3 — the create-loop gate is genuinely PER ORDER, with no per-ticker atomicity anywhere upstream or downstream. :5830-5856: for each create, `reducing = c.get("reason") == "unwind"` (:5832) — macro_probe is NOT reducing, so it IS gated; the gate is `if funding_committed + cost > min(free_cash, _total_cap()): create_skipped += 1; continue` (:5850-5853) / `elif committed + cost > _total_cap(): ... continue` (:5854-5856). `continue`, not `break`, and not keyed on ticker — so an expensive level (0.03*397 = $11.91) can be skipped while the two cheaper levels ($5.96, $2.98) pass in the same pass. Nothing restores atomicity: `cap_desired` (:3709-3759) keeps or drops WHOLE markets, `bound_creates` (:3762-3784) keeps WHOLE-ticker groups, the create-ratchet (:5833) and failed-cancel deferral (:5837-5843) are per-ticker. And cap_desired cannot pre-empt the gate because it is blind to survivors and held inventory — the code says so itself at :5810-5815/:5825 ("cap_desired only bounds the freshly-computed desired book, blind to survivors + fills"), which is exactly why `committed` at create time can sit near `_total_cap()` when cap_desired thought there was room.

STEP 4 — the resulting state is the one the module refuses to create atomically, and it lingers. `_macro_probe_quotes` returns [] for the WHOLE market when a side is unaffordable (:715-718, `return []` inside the side loop, discarding already-appended yes quotes), with the docstring stating the rationale verbatim: "Unaffordable Target -> rest NOTHING on the whole market (partial depth cannot qualify = pure fill risk; counted)" (:691-692); test_dc_macro_probe.py:75-82 pins it. A partially-rested ladder puts side depth below Target, and under the venue formula BOTH sides must meet Target or the snapshot is excluded — so the market pays nobody while our deep-discount buys still rest and can fill. Persistence: `diff_orders` (:3475-3492) matches on exact (side, price, count) and re-emits only the missing key, so the skipped level is re-attempted each cycle but stays absent while the gate binds — partial depth persists rather than self-cancelling, matching "many cycles". The regime is not exotic: the `_d3_est_ct` docstring records the walk binding at 208.4/210.25 live (:619-622), i.e. near-cap `committed` is the normal state.

Not refuted by any alternate reading I tried: macro rows never carry the "explore" key (probe_only is forced False at :2004-2009), so the :5063 clamp cannot substitute; INCUMBENT_ONLY (default 0, :996) would only make the charge $0 at :5057-5060, i.e. worse; the incumbent strip explicitly exempts macro (:5361), so the admitted market really does quote.

One point in the claimant's favour they did not state (does not change the tier): a per-order create EXCEPTION (:5871-5873) produces the same non-qualifying partial ladder without any capital pressure at all, so the partial-rest state is reachable by a second, unconditional path.

It becomes live the moment D-C is armed, which is the queued next step; the dollars at risk per occurrence are bounded (reserved $ = max loss, ~$21/side), but it silently produces a false negative in the exact receipts experiment D-C exists to run, which is decision-corrupting. Pre-arm fix, not an emergency.

**Lens reachability — CONFIRMED:** Every link in the mechanism is present in the code exactly as cited; the only correction is to the illustrative dollar figures, and the reachability is latent-only (which the claim itself states).

STEP 1 — the walk under-charges a macro ticker. `maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5043-5080` is the SELECT_BUDGET walk body. For a flat, non-exit-only, non-incumbent-stripped ticker it computes `_est9 = _kcr.est_commit_usd(...)` (:5061-5062), then `if D3_RAMP: _est9 = min(_est9, float(_d3_est_ct(_t9, ...)))` (:5067-5070). There is NO macro-probe branch anywhere in that loop — the only early-continues are held (:5046), incumbent-only/exit-only (:5057-5060). `_d3_est_ct` (:616-636) returns `D3_RUNGS[rung]` (default `[5,10,25,50]`, :529-534) with `D3_NEWSERIES_MAX_RUNG=1` (:527) clamping any series with no credits to rung ≤1, i.e. 10. Values are treated as DOLLARS at the $1/ct convention (:5068-5069 comment, :5049). So a newly designated (therefore unproven) macro ticker is charged $5 for the first `D3_RUNG_S=600s` and $10 thereafter — not the claim's flat "~$5", but the same order of magnitude. Live D3_RAMP=1 and SELECT_BUDGET=1, so this branch is the live one.

STEP 2 — the sizing exemption the walk fails to mirror is real. `_d3_apply_ramp` (:639-649): `if o.get("reason") not in ("unwind", "macro_probe") and o.get("count",0) > ramp_ct` — macro_probe quotes are explicitly NOT clamped, and this is the only ramp application site in the quote loop (:5340-5345). So the walk's ramp-based est is a valid proxy for ordinary markets and an invalid one for exactly the macro rows.

STEP 3 — the ladder's real commitment. `_macro_probe_quotes` (:681-724) builds 3 levels per side at `MACRO_PROBE_TOP..-2 ticks` with `counts = int(need*w/wsum)+1` and refuses the whole market if `cost > side_cap` where `side_cap = min(MACRO_PROBE_USD/2, 0.9*HELD_MAX_USD)` (:697, :715-718). CORRECTION to the claim: with the shipped defaults (`MACRO_PROBE_USD=60` :677, `HELD_MAX_USD=20` :1658) side_cap is $18, not $30 — and at Target=1000 the ladder costs ~$21.06/side, so it would hit `macro_probe_unaffordable` and rest NOTHING. The partial-rest scenario therefore additionally requires the operator to raise MACRO_PROBE_USD/HELD_MAX_USD, which the claim's own failure scenario concedes ("a ruled MACRO_PROBE_USD"). For any knob pair that makes the probe affordable at all, the structural gap holds: ≤$10 charged in the walk vs up to `min(MACRO_PROBE_USD/2, 0.9*HELD_MAX_USD)` per side actually committed.

STEP 4 — no downstream guard restores atomicity. I traced the full desired→create pipeline (:5745-5779): drop-grace, then `cap_desired` (:3709-3757) which keeps/cuts WHOLE markets (`_mkt_capital(desired[t])`, :3562-3563) and `break`s on breach — atomic, so it does NOT produce the partial; then `bound_creates` (:3763-3785) which keeps whole-ticker groups — also atomic. The ONLY non-atomic gate is the create loop's per-order capital check at :5844-5856 (`create_skipped += 1; continue`, not `break`), and macro quotes carry `reason="macro_probe"` (:721), so `reducing` is False (:5832) and they are gated. Critically, `cap_desired`'s running `total` does NOT include held inventory (only `fam_held` feeds the FAMILY cap, :3726-3728), while the create loop's `committed` does (`committed += held_cost`, :5825) — so a market can clear the atomic guard and then be partially skipped by the per-order gate. That divergence is the concrete reaching path, and it does not even require the walk under-charge (a single venue rejection at :5872-5875 partially rests the ladder too).

STEP 5 — the harm is as stated. Per the venue formula a side qualifies only on reaching Target, and both sides must qualify or the snapshot is excluded, so a partial ladder pays nobody while resting real bids — exactly the state `_macro_probe_quotes` refuses to create atomically (:715-718 "Unaffordable Target -> rest NOTHING"). It is also SILENT: `create_skipped` is a cycle-wide scalar with no ticker attribution (:5776, :5834-5855, :5982), and the per-market telemetry row records the DESIRED quotes `q`, not what actually rested (:5385-5387), while `macro_probe_markets` (:722-723) increments on ladder CONSTRUCTION. So the receipts study would read a full probe that never happened. `diff_orders` matches on exact side+price+count (:3475-3492), so the rested subset survives and only the missing levels retry — the partial state persists as long as capital stays tight (it does self-heal once capital frees; that is the one mitigation the claim omits).

REACHABILITY (lens): NOT reachable live. `KALSHI_MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS` is empty, so `MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS` is an empty frozenset (:675-676) and the guard `if MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS and m.get("ticker") in MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS` (:2867) short-circuits — `_macro_probe_quotes` is never called. Arming is not a hot flip either: MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS/USD/TOP are in `restart_only` (:3877-3890), so it needs an env edit plus a service restart, plus knob rulings per STEP 3. Once armed, nothing else blocks it: the designation bypasses the footprint activity/late-life/far-close drops (:2004-2009, :2041-2050, :2146-2147), the incumbent strip (:5361), and — because the macro branch returns at :2867-2870 inside `desired_quotes` (def at :2801) — the live PRESENCE_GATE (:2911-2917) and NETEV_GATE (:2953-2960) never see it.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED as a latent (dark, arm-gated) defect, with the numeric magnitudes overstated but the mechanism intact. Severity MAJOR because D-C arming is the next queued action, the fix is pre-arm and cheap, and the failure mode silently manufactures a false negative on the very experiment D-C exists to run; financial harm is bounded by MACRO_PROBE_USD by design, so it would be MINOR if D-C were shelved.

**Lens severity — CONFIRMED:** VERIFIED IN CODE, step by step (worktree kalshi-wt @ bcca876, kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py).

1) THE UNDER-CHARGE IS REAL AND UNCONDITIONAL WHEN ARMED. The select-budget walk computes a market's est at :5061-5062 (`_kcr.est_commit_usd(ref, MAX_MARKET_CAPITAL=250 :239, INV_HARD_CT=80 :1555)` → ~$80 at an unknown 0.50 ref, per kalshi_capital_rank.py:80-103), then at :5067-5070 does `_est9 = min(_est9, float(_d3_est_ct(_t9, now)))`. There is NO macro exemption at that line — I read the whole walk (:5015-5101) and grepped every `MACRO_PROBE` site in the file (617-724, 1312-1323, 2004-2050, 2146, 2867-2870, 3886-3889, 5070, 5361): the walk is the only macro-relevant capital site with no designation branch. `_d3_est_ct` (:617-636) returns D3_RUNGS[rung] = [5,10,25,50] (:529-534), rung 0 = 5 for a first-seen ticker, and D3_NEWSERIES_MAX_RUNG=1 (:527, :631-635) PERMANENTLY clamps an unproven series (credits_n==0) to rung 1 = 10. A macro-probe ticker is by construction an unproven low-competition pool, so its walk charge is $5→$10 forever, not just for the first 600s. D3_RAMP=1 is LIVE, so the min() is active.

2) THE SIZING EXEMPTION IS REAL, SO THE CHARGE IS WRONG BY CONSTRUCTION. `_d3_apply_ramp` (:639-650) skips any order whose `reason` is "unwind" or "macro_probe" — the macro ladder is never rung-clamped, and the quote loop's ramp call (:5322-5346) is the only ramp application. `_macro_probe_quotes` (:681-724) rests 3 levels/side at prices [0.03,0.02,0.01] (MACRO_PROBE_TOP=0.03 :678, TICK=0.01 :1565) with weights [0.4,0.3,0.3] and counts `int(need*w)+1` (:711-713). Exact cost per side = 0.021·need + 0.06. At the venue Target of 1000ct on an empty book that is $21.06/side, $42.12/market, vs $5–10 charged in the walk — a 4.2×–8.4× under-read. (The claim's "~$30/side" needs MACRO_PROBE_USD=60 AND HELD_MAX_USD≳33; with the default HELD_MAX_USD=20 :1658 the side cap is min(30, 18)=$18 (:697) and a full-Target empty book is instead REFUSED at :715-718. So the claim's dollar figure is knob-dependent, but the direction and the ≥4× magnitude hold under any arming that lets the ladder rest at all.)

3) THE PER-ORDER CAPITAL GATE IS GENUINELY NON-ATOMIC, AND ITS BINDING CONDITION IS NOT EXOTIC. Create loop :5830-5856: `cost` is per order, the gate is `committed + cost > _total_cap()` (:5854, FUNDING_GATE default 0 :1464 and absent from live.env), and the failure action is `continue`, not `break` — so the loop keeps placing CHEAPER later orders after skipping an expensive earlier one. `committed` = surviving standing notional (:5813-5815) + held_cost (:5825). cap_desired (:3709-3759) already bounded the DESIRED book by `_total_cap()` using the TRUE ladder dollars (`_mkt_capital` = Σ price×count, :3562-3563) — but it starts `total=0` and never adds held_cost. So the gate bites by exactly the held_cost overhang whenever the book is packed near cap, which is the normal packed state (the code's own live figure: "$346.78" resting, :3574). Within a ticker the ladder's order survives diff_orders (:3489-3491) and the stable unwind-first sort (:5829), but tickers are interleaved by set iteration (:3481) and bound_creates returns unchanged when under WRITE_BUDGET=400 (:3766-3767), so the skipped set is value-blind and essentially arbitrary.

4) THE CLAIMED FAILURE FOLLOWS, AND TWO CONSEQUENCES ARE WORSE THAN CLAIMED. (a) A partial ladder is exactly the state `_macro_probe_quotes` refuses to create atomically (:691-692, :715-718) — the side misses Target, the snapshot is excluded, we pay fill risk for zero reward: the design's risk-for-reward trade inverts to pure risk. (b) Because the loop continues past a skip, the surviving subset can be the 0.01 level alone — materializing precisely the 1c-only wall the D-F ruling forbids and that :707-710 (`macro_probe_wall_refused`) exists to prevent. (c) The backstop alarm's documented remedy misfires: `budget_backstop_fired` (:5749-5756) tells the operator to LOWER KALSHI_SELECT_BUDGET_MARGIN, which starves the WHOLE book to compensate for a macro-specific mis-charge. And the under-charge quantitatively wipes the live safety margin: SELECT_BUDGET_MARGIN=-0.3 sets the walk limit to 0.7·cap (:5020); two designated tickers under-read by ~$37 each ≈ $74 ≈ 30% of a $245 limit on a ~$350 cap — the entire headroom the margin buys.

5) REFUTATION ATTEMPTS THAT PARTLY MITIGATE (why this is not UPGRADE). cap_desired keeps WHOLE markets (:3731-3755) and bound_creates keeps whole-ticker groups (:3762-3784) — both truncation layers ARE atomic and re-rank by alloc_prio, so the most common outcome of an over-admitted low-priority macro market is a safe whole-market drop, not a partial rest. The HELD_MAX_USD level breaker (:4978 `risk_cost > HELD_MAX_USD` → whole book reduce-only, :5614) bounds the held overhang that drives the gate. `need` subtracts only RIVAL depth (:700-702), so the desired ladder stays the full Target ladder every cycle and the diff re-creates missing levels once capital frees — the partial state is not permanently latched. Per-occurrence dollar exposure is bounded by side_cap ($18 at defaults, $30 at the claim's knobs) at 1–3c prices where a fill is far below fair. Also note partial ladders can already arise from per-order create REJECTIONS (:5871-5891) independent of this defect.

6) REACHABILITY vs LIVE ENV. LATENT-ONLY today: KALSHI_MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS is empty, so `MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS` is an empty frozenset (:675-676) and every macro branch (:2004, :2867, :5361) is dead. It becomes live the moment D-C is armed with a coherent MACRO_PROBE_USD/HELD_MAX_USD pair — i.e. this is an ARM-GATE item, not a live-now bleed. SELECT_BUDGET=1, MARGIN=-0.3, D3_RAMP=1 are all live, so no other flag flip is needed.

SEVERITY: I hold MAJOR, but for the arm gate rather than for direct dollars. The certain part (walk charges $5-10 for a market that commits up to $42; -0.3 margin erased) is unconditional whenever D-C is armed; the partial-rest path is a conjunction (armed + knobs that let the ladder rest + book packed to cap + nonzero held_cost + ladder orders late in create order) with small absolute dollars (≤ ~$21-30/side, deep-discount, max loss = reserved $). What earns MAJOR is that the failure silently invalidates the ONE receipts experiment D-C exists to run — the operator would read "macro probes don't pay" from a book that never met Target — and, with the live $10 daily loss halt, a single ~$21 adverse macro fill exceeds the day's halt budget while having bought no qualifying snapshot. Not UPGRADE: the two atomic layers above the gate genuinely reduce frequency and the exposure is bounded. Not DOWNGRADE: the accounting error itself is certain, quantified, and defeats a deliberately-set live safety margin.
